**Pre-dispatch attack** (architect, under `.claude/skills/acceptance/` "Have it attacked before dispatch": items depend on a system outside Rigger's code). Every citation was opened on `origin/main` at 280ec1bf. The card asks for exact timed commands, full output for failed runs, and every pid recorded at its start. Its items 16–23 under "Acceptance" make each of those ruleable. The findings follow.

1. **The integrity label can be read from the wrong process.** Items 4 and 5 accept "the mandatory label of the supervisor's own process **or of the session that started it**". A filtered or other-account token, which is exactly what this card has to obtain, is a property of the launched process, and the parent shell's label does not prove it. #1430's own route ran `whoami /groups` in the session ("The user and elevation"). **Fix:** both items read the label from the supervisor process's own token, during the run.
2. **E2's result can be the SSH session's job, not the spike's.** #1430 found that the session's own job ended every member on disconnect ("Was Node already in a job"; "Native Windows", "The next card must not assume"). Item 13 asks whether the session was still open, but nothing stops a run with the session closed from counting toward item 14's conclusion. **Fix:** add "Each E2 run the report counts toward its conclusion had the session that started the supervisor open when the post-kill listing was taken, as the transcript shows." Report any other E2 run as unattributed.
3. **Nothing says what done means if no non-elevated token is obtained.** #1430's `runas /trustlevel:0x20000` failed, and Windows OpenSSH gives an administrator a full token. If the owner declines an account (see "Owner decisions") and no filtered token works, every "Given … ran non-elevated" item is vacuously met, and item 14 cannot be answered. **Fix:** add an item quoting each attempt to obtain a non-elevated token, with its full output. Then state what the card owes when none worked: the report answers "not measured" with those attempts, or the card escalates as `ambiguous`. The PM chooses which.
4. **The account gate is in the wrong place.** "The setup" gates the first *run* under a new account, and item 3 says "posted before the first run under that account". Creating the account is itself the host-configuration change #1430 names as the owner's to approve ("Questions this leaves open"). **Fix:** "creates no account until rigger-ae relays the owner's approval on this card". In item 3, change "before the first run under that account" to "before the account was created".
5. **Survival has no baseline.** Item 9's pre-ending listing shows whether each member is in the job, but not that each member is alive. Without that, a "not alive" reading after the ending is not shown to be a change. **Fix:** the pre-ending listing also shows each fixture member alive.
6. **Two sibling rules bind at once.** "Rules on how it runs" binds #1429's rules "as written there, with this card's number in place of #1429's". That carries in #1429's "Sibling spikes share the WSL2 machine", which says to post on "all three cards", now the closed #1429–#1431. This card's own "Siblings share hosts" bullet says to post on "each sibling card" and names only "the E1 spikes". A maker cannot tell which set to wait for. **Fix:** say this card's bullet replaces #1429's rule, and name the sibling set as #1458, #1459, #1460, #1462 and #1463. All five may touch the WSL2 or Windows machine.
7. **"Every process it starts" has no stated scope.** It is unclear whether it covers the fixture members the fixture spawns, the supervisor, one-shot commands such as a listing or `ssh`, and processes run under another account. Each reading gives a different process record, and so a different ruling on "Each entry in the process record carries a recorded time earlier than…". **Fix:** define it as every process still running when the command that started it returned, plus the supervisor and every fixture member. A process that a spike process spawns may be recorded by the process that spawns it, at the moment it spawns it. A command that exits before returning is covered by its transcript entry.
8. **The final sweep can pass on a reused pid, or fail on one.** "a process listing taken after its last run in which none of the recorded pids is alive" reads pids alone, and Windows reuses them quickly. **Fix:** record each process's creation time beside its pid, and have the sweep match on both.

**Owner decisions**

- Whether the spike may create a standard Windows account on the Windows machine (`hostpc`). Findings 3 and 4 depend on the answer, and the architect does not rule on it.

Temporal spike: whether a non-elevated Windows supervisor can end an agent's whole tree through a Job Object
<!-- ABOUTME: Spike card: whether a non-elevated Windows supervisor can hold an agent's whole process tree in a Job Object and end it, including after the supervisor is killed. -->
- **Kind:** `spike`. Maker `spikeEngineer` (`rigger.config.mjs`, `kinds.spike`). Its code is throwaway and never merges (`AGENTS.md`, "When you write code").
- **Dispatch:** by hand, by the rigger-ae coordinator. It runs beside M8 and touches nothing of M8's; no M8 card waits for it.
- **Branch:** `rigger-<card>`, for the report's docs-only pull request.
- **Lands in:**
  - `docs/spikes/<report>.md`, the report.
  - `docs/spikes/<report>/`, the evidence the report cites.
  - `docs/journal/<date>-<time>-<card>-<slug>.md`, its journal entry.
  - `doc-references.json`, the report's entry.
  - `spikes/`, gitignored: the throwaway work, never committed.
- **Attacked:** pending: pre-dispatch attack.
- **Authorized:** the owner, in an interactive session on 2026-11-21, ruling the four epics, relayed by rigger-ae at [1453#7072953923](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1453#issuecomment-7072953923).

Part of the **Temporal** track, epic #1454 (whole-tree containment). **Author:** the PM.

**The track.** The Temporal track is a post-v0 exploration; its record is #1453. Three spikes (#1429, #1430, #1431) measured what Temporal gives a card's dispatch and what it does not. #1453 recommends going ahead after v0 on condition, in this order: whole-tree containment on every OS (E1), a design that stops a second attempt running alongside the first (E2), and only then the Temporal runtime (E3) and the board as a mirror (E4). The owner ruled the four epics on 2026-11-21 ([1453#7072953923](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1453#issuecomment-7072953923)). Cards are filed just in time; this one is in its epic's first wave. Nothing here records a decision, amends a requirement or changes `ARCHITECTURE.md` or `README.md`; a finding that calls for one is a proposal for the PM, the architect and the owner.

---

**The question.** Can a Windows supervisor that runs without elevation (below High Mandatory Level) create a Job Object, set kill-on-job-close on it, assign a dispatch's root to it, and so end the dispatch's whole tree, both when the supervisor ends the job and when the supervisor is itself killed outright?

**Why a spike.** #1430 measured Job Objects only from an elevated supervisor. Windows OpenSSH gave the administrator's session a full token, and the attempt at a reduced token failed, so no non-elevated run was made (`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment.md`, "The user and elevation"). Its report names a non-elevated supervisor that cannot create or assign the job as something that would reverse its Windows recommendation ("Native Windows: a Job Object per dispatch, with kill-on-job-close"). Whether the calls succeed at a lower integrity level, and whether a job the host already put the supervisor in permits a nested one, are properties of the host.

**What exists already, as context that earns no credit.**

- #1430's Windows fixture and supervisor: `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture-win/`, and its members M1 to M4 and M6 ("The fixture"). The spike may reuse them, and says if it changed them.
- #1430's elevated results, x2 to x4: `TerminateJobObject` ended every member, and with kill-on-job-close set, `taskkill /F` of the supervisor ended every member ("The table").
- #1430's caution: under SSH, the session's own job ended every member on disconnect ("Native Windows", "The next card must not assume"). A member ended that way was not ended by the spike's job.

**The setup.**

- **Host:** the Windows machine (`hostpc`).
- **A non-elevated token.** The spike chooses how it obtains one, for example a standard user account or an administrator's filtered token, and says why. The owner ruled that the spike may create **one dedicated local standard (non-admin) account** on the Windows machine, records the account, and deletes it when the spike ends ([1453#7073093965](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1453#issuecomment-7073093965), ruling 2). The spike names that account in its stated-intent comment and creates no account before posting it.
- **If no non-elevated token can be obtained,** the card is still done: the report answers the question "not measured", with every attempt it made and that attempt's full output, and the items that begin "Given … non-elevated" are met by that answer.
- **The endings,** as #1430 named them: E1, the supervisor ends the job with `TerminateJobObject`; E2, the supervisor is killed outright with `taskkill /F` or `Stop-Process -Force` while the tree runs, with kill-on-job-close set.
- **Survival** is read at least 10 s after an ending, the bound #1430 used.

**Scope edges.** Elevated runs (#1430 measured them), a job without kill-on-job-close and the recovery of an orphaned job (sibling card #1460), a breakaway flag, and a real agent session are outside this card.

**Hosts, and whose custody each is under.** #1429's section "Hosts, and whose custody each is under" binds this card as written there, except for the Mac mini's rule, which is below: the WSL2 machine and the Windows machine as the primary hosts and how each is reached, and the laptop's list of what it never takes. #1430's report found that `wsl-hostpc` did not answer and reached WSL through `ssh hostpc [name withheld] -e …` (`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment.md`, "What ran, and where"). Any use of the Mac mini goes through the rigger-d1 coordinator first: the spike asks rigger-ae on this card, waits for rigger-ae to relay rigger-d1's OK, and runs every command there through the mini's suite-slot script (`tools/coordinator/suite-slot.sh` in this repository), at the path and with the environment rigger-d1's terms give at dispatch, so that it takes the same slot M8's work does.

**Rules on how it runs.** #1429's "Rules on how it runs" binds this card as written there, with this card's number in place of #1429's, except its "Sibling spikes share the WSL2 machine" rule, which the "Siblings share hosts" bullet below replaces. Three of #1429's rules cost the last three spikes several judge rounds, so this card tightens them:

- **Exact commands.** The transcript holds every command exactly as it ran, character for character, with its UTC time and host. A command written up from memory, or condensed, is not a transcript entry. A timed figure names the transcript entries of the commands that produced both its endpoints.
- **Failed runs in full.** A run that failed, was abandoned or was repeated keeps its full, unabridged output under `docs/spikes/<report>/`. The report says why each one failed or was abandoned.
- **Every pid, recorded at its start.** The processes that count are the supervisor, every fixture member, and every other process still running when the command that started it returned, on any host and under any account, the dedicated account's processes included. A process that a spike process spawns may be recorded by the process that spawns it, at the moment it spawns it. A command that exits before it returns is covered by its transcript entry, and needs no record entry.
  - Each entry in the process record, under `docs/spikes/<report>/`, carries the process's host, its pid, its creation time as the host reports it, and the UTC time the entry was written. The entry is written as the process starts and before the next command on that host.
  - A process is the recorded one only where both its pid and its creation time match an entry; a pid alone is not a match, because Windows reuses pids quickly.
  - A pid found afterwards in a listing is a disclosure, not a record. The spike ends only recorded processes and their descendants (`AGENTS.md`, "End only recorded processes").
- **Siblings share hosts.** The sibling set is #1458, #1459, #1460, #1462 and #1463, and any of them may touch the WSL2 machine or the Windows machine while this card runs. Before a WSL restart, a `.wslconfig` or `/etc/wsl.conf` change, a logout of a user's sessions, or any other step that ends processes it did not start, the spike posts the step on each other card in that set and waits for each one's spike, or rigger-ae, to answer that none of its recorded processes would be ended.

## Acceptance

- The report gives the Windows build, the Node version and the version of each package the supervisor loaded, each quoted from the command that printed it.
- The report states how the non-elevated token was obtained, naming the account the supervisor ran as.
- The report names the account it created under the owner's ruling, and links its stated-intent comment, posted before the account was created, or states that it created none.
- Where the spike created an account, the report quotes the command that deleted it at the end, and a listing of the machine's local accounts taken afterwards that does not show it.
- The report quotes each attempt it made to obtain a non-elevated token, with that attempt's full output.
- Where no attempt yielded a supervisor below High Mandatory Level, the report answers the card's question "not measured", naming those attempts.
- For each run the report counts as non-elevated, the report quotes the mandatory label of the supervisor process's own token, read from that process during that run.
- For each run the report counts as non-elevated, the quoted mandatory label is below High Mandatory Level.
- The report quotes whether the supervisor's process was already in a job before it created one, as the call it used returned it.
- For each non-elevated run, the report quotes the return value, and the error code where the call failed, of each call that creates the job, sets kill-on-job-close and assigns the dispatch's root.
- Where any of those calls failed in every non-elevated run, the report states which endings could not run as a result.
- Given the job was assigned, the report quotes a listing before any ending that shows, for each fixture member, whether it is in the spike's job.
- Given the job was assigned, the report quotes a listing taken immediately before each ending that shows each fixture member alive.
- Given E1 ran non-elevated, the report quotes a listing taken at least 10 s after `TerminateJobObject` that shows, for each fixture member, whether it is alive.
- Given E1 ran non-elevated, the report gives the measured time from the `TerminateJobObject` call to the job reporting no live process, or states that it did not within a bound the report states.
- Given E2 ran non-elevated with kill-on-job-close set, the report quotes a listing taken at least 10 s after the supervisor was killed that shows, for each fixture member, whether it is alive.
- For each E2 run, the report states, citing the transcript, whether the session that started the supervisor was still open when the post-kill listing was taken.
- Each E2 run the report counts toward its conclusion had the session that started the supervisor open when the post-kill listing was taken, as the transcript shows; the report names any other E2 run as unattributed.
- The report states whether a non-elevated supervisor can end a dispatch's whole tree through a Job Object, for E1 and for E2 separately, on the evidence above.
- The report says what evidence would reverse that recommendation.
- The report gives the path of the spike's full transcript under `docs/spikes/<report>/`, in which every command carries its UTC time and host.
- Every command the report quotes appears in the transcript character for character as it ran.
- Every figure the report marks as measured names the transcript entries of the commands that produced its endpoints.
- Every figure the report gives that is not measured is marked as judgment, with its premise.
- For every run that failed, was abandoned or was repeated, the report gives the path of that run's full, unabridged output under `docs/spikes/<report>/`, and says why it failed or was abandoned.
- The report gives the path of a process record under `docs/spikes/<report>/` listing every process that counts under "Every pid, recorded at its start", each with its host, pid, creation time and the UTC time it was recorded.
- Each entry in the process record carries a recorded time earlier than the transcript's next command on that host after the process started.
- For each host the spike used, the report quotes a process listing taken after its last run, showing each process's pid and creation time, in which no process matches a record entry on both.
- The report quotes the command it ran over every file it committed under `docs/spikes/<report>/` to find credentials and tokens, and its result.
- The report lists every file the spike wrote outside its own directories, on every host, each with its reason and whether it was removed, or says there were none.
- The report links the stated-intent comment on this card, posted before the spike's first install or host-configuration change.
- The report lists every host-configuration change the spike made, each with its host and whether it was reverted, or says there were none.
- The report states whether the spike used the Mac mini, and if it did, links the comment relaying rigger-d1's OK, posted before its first use.
- The report names each recorded decision, requirement or `ARCHITECTURE.md` section its recommendation would change, as a proposal, and the pull request changes none of them.

ABOUTME: Spike findings for card #1430: whether cgroup v2 under WSL2 and Job Objects on Windows end an
agent's whole process tree, measured against the macOS baseline and today's route.

# Whole-tree containment on Linux under WSL2 and on Windows

## The answer

On Linux under WSL2, the answer is yes. An unprivileged supervisor obtained a cgroup it could write
to from the systemd user manager, with no root. Writing cgroup.kill ended all six fixture
members, including the two that left the session and the one that moved to `/`. The cgroup reported
no live process between 0.88 and 1.66 ms after the write, in the three runs that measured it. When the supervisor was
killed outright, all six members lived on in the orphaned cgroup. A fresh process that wrote
cgroup.kill to that cgroup ended every one of them.

On native Windows, the answer is also yes, with one condition. A Node supervisor reached Job Objects
...

agent's whole process tree, measured against the macOS baseline and today's route.

# Whole-tree containment on Linux under WSL2 and on Windows

## The answer

On Linux under WSL2, the answer is yes. An unprivileged supervisor obtained a cgroup it could write
to from the systemd user manager, with no root. Writing cgroup.kill ended all six fixture
members, including the two that left the session and the one that moved to `/`. The cgroup reported
no live process between 0.88 and 1.66 ms after the write, in the three runs that measured it. When the supervisor was
killed outright, all six members lived on in the orphaned cgroup. A fresh process that wrote
cgroup.kill to that cgroup ended every one of them.

On native Windows, the answer is also yes, with one condition. A Node supervisor reached Job Objects
through koffi, an FFI package with a prebuilt binary. Every member it started, M1 to M4 and M6, was
in its job. `TerminateJobObject` ended all of them. With kill-on-job-close set, killing the
supervisor with `taskkill /F` ended every member too. Without kill-on-job-close, every member
outlived the supervisor. A fresh process could not then reach the job, because its name disappeared
with the supervisor's last handle. The condition is that this supervisor ran elevated (High
Mandatory Level). A non-elevated supervisor was not measured.

Today's route (`R-STATE-17`) left M6 alive on both macOS and Linux. M6 is the member that starts a
new session and changes its working directory to `/`. The group kill and the working-directory
census found every other member. On macOS, the process-group kill alone also left M3 alive, the
member that starts a new session. Killing the supervisor outright left every member alive on macOS,
as `R-STATE-9` and `R-STATE-10` expect.

Round 2 repeated every measurement on every host with every process the runs started recorded by
pid, as the judge's round-1 verdict asked. Each repeat gave the same result for every member
("Round 2", below).

I recommend a delegated cgroup ended by cgroup.kill for Linux under WSL2, and a Job Object with
kill-on-job-close for native Windows. Both are under "Recommendations", with what would reverse
each. This report hands over evidence. It changes no code, requirement or recorded decision.

WSL was reached only through `ssh hostpc [name withheld] -e …`, because `wsl-hostpc` did not answer. In every

of the user's systemd manager (pid 339), as each listing's parent pid column shows. It was never a
Windows process.

## The fixture

The fixture is one process tree started in a dispatch directory the spike made: `mac/d-<run>` in
the laptop's spike directory, `~/1430-spike-engineer/d-<run>` in WSL, and
`C:\Users\user\1430-spike-engineer\d-<run>` on Windows. Each member sleeps 300 s unless ended. On
macOS and Linux, `argv[0]` is `fx-<run>-M<n>`. On Windows, the command line carries
`fx1430-<run>-M<n>`.

### How each member was started

| Member | macOS and Linux (`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/fixture.sh`, run by `bash` as the supervised command) | Windows (`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture-win/fixture-win.cjs`, run by `node` as the supervised command) |
|---|---|---|
| M1 | `bash -c "(exec -a fx-R-M2 sleep 300) & exec -a fx-R-M1 sleep 300" &`: a direct child of the supervised command | `spawn(node, [member-win.cjs, …-M1, 'M1'])`: a direct child |
| M2 | the subshell M1 forks before it execs, a grandchild whose parent is M1 | M1 runs `spawn(node, [member-win.cjs, …-M2, 'leaf'])`, a grandchild whose parent is M1 |
| M3 | `perl -MPOSIX -e 'POSIX::setsid(); exec {"sleep"} "fx-R-M3", "300"' &`: a new session | `spawn(node, […-M3], { detached: true })`. libuv's src/win/process.c (read at `v1.51.0`) makes this `DETACHED_PROCESS \| CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP`, a new process group |
| M4 | `bash -c "(exec -a fx-R-M4 sleep 300) & exit 0" &`: its parent exits at once, so it is reparented | `cmd.exe /d /c start "" /b node member-win.cjs …-M4 leaf`. Its parent cmd.exe exits at once |
| M5 | `bash -c "trap '' TERM; exec -a fx-R-M5 sleep 300" &`: `SIGTERM` is ignored, and `SIG_IGN` survives `exec` | not applicable: `TerminateProcess` cannot be ignored |
| M6 | `perl -MPOSIX -e 'POSIX::setsid(); chdir "/"; exec {"sleep"} "fx-R-M6", "300"' &`: a new session working in `/` | `spawn(node, […-M6, 'M6'], { detached: true })`. The member runs `process.chdir()` to the drive root, `C:\` |

The supervised command waits on stdin for one line before it starts any member. The supervisor moves
it into the cgroup or job first, and then writes the line. That closes the race between spawning
and assigning. Every supervisor spawned the supervised command with `detached: true`, as
`runCommand` in `src/substrate/process.mjs` does, so that command leads a process group of its own.

### The supervisors

- **macOS and Linux:** `fixture/supervisor.cjs <e1|e1c|e2> <run> <dispatch> <fixture-dir>`.
  - On Linux, it reads its own cgroup from `/proc/self/cgroup` and creates a child cgroup `tree`
    under it. It writes the supervised command's pid into tree/cgroup.procs.
  - E1 on Linux writes `1` to tree/cgroup.kill. It then polls tree/cgroup.events every 1 ms
    until it reads `populated 0`.
  - E1 on macOS sends `SIGKILL` to the process group.
  - E1c, on both, sends `SIGKILL` to the process group. It then runs
    `lsof -w -n -P -a -d cwd -u <uid> -F pun`, the argument list `listing` in
    `src/substrate/process.mjs` builds, and sends `SIGKILL` to every pid whose working directory
    lies under the dispatch directory's real path.
  - It lists 3 s after the start and 12 s after the ending.
- **Windows:** `fixture-win/supervisor-win.cjs <e1|e2k|e2n> fx1430-<run>-sup <dispatch>`.
  - It creates a job named `Local\fx1430-<run>` with `CreateJobObjectW`. For e1 and e2k, it sets
    `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE` with `SetInformationJobObject(JobObjectExtendedLimitInformation)`.
    For e2n it leaves the limit unset.
  - It assigns the supervised command with `AssignProcessToJobObject`.
  - E1 calls `TerminateJobObject`. It then polls `JobObjectBasicAccountingInformation.ActiveProcesses`
    until the count is 0.
  - It lists 5 s after the start and 12 s after the ending.
- **The listings:**
  - `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/list.sh` uses `ps` for pid, parent pid, group and state, and os.getsid for the
    session. It takes the working directory from `/proc/<pid>/cwd` on Linux and from `lsof -d cwd`
    on macOS, and the cgroup from `/proc/<pid>/cgroup` on Linux.
  - `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture-win/win32.cjs` uses `Win32_Process` for pid, parent pid and session. It calls
    `IsProcessInJob` against the spike's job and against any job. It reads the working directory
    from the process's PEB (`RTL_USER_PROCESS_PARAMETERS.CurrentDirectory`).

## Native Windows

### The user and elevation (00:56:09Z)

`whoami` printed `host-pc10\user` at 00:41:04Z. `whoami /groups`, run in an SSH session as the user
that ran every supervisor, printed the following. The three lines below are quoted with their whitespace condensed. The full output is in the
transcript, with one account line redacted:

```
[name withheld] Alias            S-1-5-32-544   Mandatory group, Enabled by default, Enabled group, Group owner
NT AUTHORITY\Local account and member of Administrators group Well-known group S-1-5-114      Mandatory group, Enabled by default, Enabled group
Mandatory Label\High Mandatory Level                          Label            S-1-16-12288
```

The supervisor ran elevated. Windows OpenSSH gave this administrator's session a full token at
High integrity. An attempt to run a reduced token with `runas /trustlevel:0x20000` failed at
00:58:20Z with `The system cannot find the path specified.`, so no non-elevated run was made.

### Routes from Node to a Job Object

| Route | What it installs or builds | Weighed |
|---|---|---|
| **koffi** (FFI), used | `npm install koffi` added 2 packages in the spike's directory: `koffi@3.3.8` and the prebuilt @koromix/koffi-win32-x64. Nothing was compiled. npm 11.17.0 did not run koffi's install script, which was not yet covered by `allowScripts`, and the prebuilt binary loaded without it | Calls `kernel32` and `ntdll` directly from the supervisor's own process, so the job handle lives and dies with the supervisor. That is the property E2 measures. Adds a native dependency per platform |
| A node-gyp native addon | Visual Studio Build Tools with the C++ workload, and Python, on every host that builds it. It builds a .node binary | The same in-process handle as koffi, at the cost of a compiler toolchain on the host. Not built |
| A PowerShell or C# helper process (`Add-Type` P/Invoke) spawned by Node | Nothing: PowerShell ships with Windows | The job handle lives in the helper, not the supervisor, so killing the supervisor tests the helper's lifetime instead. Every call also pays a PowerShell start. Not built |

### Was Node already in a job (00:56:46Z)

Before it created a job, the supervisor called `IsProcessInJob(GetCurrentProcess(), NULL, &r)`:

```
2026-11-20T00:56:46.318Z IsProcessInJob(GetCurrentProcess(), NULL) before creating a job: returned 1, Result=TRUE (already in a job)
```

The SSH session's processes already sat in a job. After x4, with the session closed, the members x4
left had ended. That is evidence that the session's job ends its members on disconnect. Every
reading below was taken inside one SSH session, before it closed.

### Before any ending, with job membership

```
listing run=x2 at 2026-11-20T00:56:51.733Z on Windows (Win32_Process + IsProcessInJob + PEB CurrentDirectory)

`IN_SPIKE_JOB` is `IsProcessInJob(member, spike job)`. M1 to M4 and M6 each read `yes`, and the
supervisor reads `no`. The job's own process list also showed them, with two conhost.exe the

job process id list (JobObjectBasicProcessIdList): ["1304:conhost.exe(ppid 21128)","1480:node.exe(ppid 30952)","8140:conhost.exe(ppid 37664)","36504:node.exe(ppid 30952)","32708:node.exe(ppid 37664)","30952:node.exe(ppid 2652)","21128:node.exe(ppid 30952)","36552:node.exe(ppid 21128)"] accounting: {"total":9,"active":8,"terminated":0}
```

M4's parent, 37664, is the cmd.exe that had already exited. x1, x3 and x4 show the same
membership. Their listings are in the transcript at 00:56:14Z, 00:57:10Z and 00:57:33Z.

### The job's limits in each run

| Run | Logged limit line |
|---|---|
| x1, E1 | `LimitFlags=0x2000 KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE=set` |
| x2, E1 | `LimitFlags=0x2000 KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE=set` |

Each row is read back with `QueryInformationJobObject(JobObjectExtendedLimitInformation)`. No
breakaway limit was set in any run.

### E2 with kill-on-job-close set (x3)

The runner killed the supervisor with `taskkill /F /PID 32600` at 00:57:16.324Z, and `taskkill`
printed `SUCCESS`. Twelve seconds later, a fresh process listed:

```
listing run=x3 at 2026-11-20T00:57:28.808Z on Windows (Win32_Process + IsProcessInJob + PEB CurrentDirectory)
PID     PPID    SESS  IN_SPIKE_JOB IN_ANY_JOB CWD | ROLE
count=0
```

M1, M2, M3, M4 and M6 had all ended. The supervisor's handle was the job's last, so closing it ended
the job. That same process's `OpenJobObjectW` returned `NULL` with `GetLastError=2`.

### E2 without kill-on-job-close (x4)

`taskkill /F /PID 2092` ran at 00:57:39.980Z. Twelve seconds later:

**Could a fresh process reach the orphaned job?** No. The fresh process, pid 9496, called
`OpenJobObjectW(JOB_OBJECT_ALL_ACCESS, FALSE, "Local\fx1430-x4")`:

2026-11-20T00:57:52.049Z fresh process pid 9496: OpenJobObjectW(JOB_OBJECT_ALL_ACCESS, FALSE, "Local\fx1430-x4") -> NULL, GetLastError=2

Error 2 is `ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND`. The job still held five live members, but its name went with the
supervisor's handle, the last one open. The runner's call to `TerminateJobObject` was gated on the
open succeeding, so it never ran. Ten seconds later, the members were unchanged:

Holding a handle in a second process, which would keep the name open, was not tried. The members

## macOS, the baseline

### 6. 2026-11-22T02:54:58.943Z -> 2026-11-22T02:54:59.070Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Read round 2, recommendations and custody of #1430"}

## Round 2: every measurement repeated, with every process recorded

The round-1 judge found that round 1 recorded only the long-lived processes. It never recorded the
pids of short-lived helpers, or of the intermediate process that M4's parent is on macOS and Linux
("Every process the spike started", below). Those pids cannot be recovered, so round 2 repeated
every card measurement, on every host, on 2026-11-20, with every process the runs started
recorded. The stated intent for the new steps went up first, at
[1430#7044905230](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1430#issuecomment-7044905230).

### How each process was recorded

| Host | What recorded it |
|---|---|
| All | Every Node process loads `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/rec.cjs`. It writes the process's own pid and parent, and the pid, command and exit of every process it starts, as JSON lines. The laptop's step runner, `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/orch.cjs`, records each `ssh` and local command the same way, and writes `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/transcript-r2.txt`: each step's exact argv, its stdin script verbatim with sha256, its UTC start and end, and its full output |
| macOS and Linux | `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/fixture.sh` writes the pid of every process it starts, M4's parent included, using bash builtins only. The listing (`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/list.cjs`) runs in Node, with one recorded `ps`. On macOS it adds one recorded `lsof -O` and one recorded `python3`, run by its real path rather than through the `/usr/bin/python3` stub |
| WSL | Each script fed to `bash -s` writes its own pid and its two WSL init parents, and runs every external command through a `rec` function that prints the command's pid, using bash builtins only. Each supervisor's unit prints the pid of its `id` and of its own bash, which then execs Node |
| Windows | A watcher, `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/watcher.ps1`, ran in its own SSH session from 16:34:25Z to 16:41:43Z. It logged every process start and stop on the machine through WMI's `Win32_ProcessStartTrace` and `Win32_ProcessStopTrace`. `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/collect-win.cjs` then picks out the spike's own processes: the watcher's session chain, every per-connection `sshd` that started within 3 s of one of the laptop's recorded `ssh` spawns, and every descendant of either. The result is `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/win-mine-full.json`. The log holds 12 new per-connection `sshd` processes, from 16:35:03Z to 16:41:19Z, and each one matched one of the spike's 12 `ssh` spawns in that span. No other connection arrived while the watcher ran. Every pid that `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/rec.cjs` recorded on Windows is in that set |

On Windows every file sat in one directory, so the sources under
`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/win/` loaded a byte-identical copy of
`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/rec.cjs` beside them. `cmp` confirmed the two were
identical, and the copy is not committed twice.

Round 2 differs from round 1 only in how it records:

- The census and the macOS listing call `lsof -O`, so lsof does its kernel reads in its own
  process instead of forked helpers. The census reads the same fields as before.
- `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/fixture.sh` ends with `exec sleep 300` instead of `sleep 300`, so its tail makes no new process.
- The listing runs in Node instead of `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/list.sh`.
- WSL was reached only through `ssh hostpc [name withheld] -e`, as in round 1. The WSL machine booted again
  between sessions, because WSL stops when idle. The final check printed a boot time of 16:41:18Z,
  and its pids began again near 400.

### What round 2 measured

Every cell of the table below matches round 1. Times are in `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/transcript-r2.txt`.

| Run | Ending | Reading at least 10 s after the ending |
|---|---|---|
| mr1, macOS | E1, process-group kill of 74511 at 16:33:16.190Z | count=3: the supervisor, M3 (74528) and M6 (74532) alive. M1, M2, M4 and M5 ended |
| mr2, macOS | E1c, group kill of 99236, then the census killed 99254 (M3) | count=2: the supervisor and M6 (99267) alive. M1 to M5 ended |
| mr3, macOS | E2, SIGKILL of supervisor 21189 at 16:33:49.272Z | count=7: the supervised command and M1 to M6 all alive |
| wr1, WSL2 | E1, cgroup.kill, `populated 0` after 0.88 ms | count=1: the supervisor only. M1 to M6 ended |
| wr2, WSL2 | E1c, group kill of 651, then the census killed 653 (M3) | count=2: the supervisor and M6 (656) alive. M1 to M5 ended |
| wr3, WSL2 | E2, SIGKILL of supervisor 666 at 16:37:54.154Z | count=7: the supervised command and M1 to M6 alive in the orphaned cgroup. The fresh writer, pid 685, wrote cgroup.kill, which read `populated 0` after 0.43 ms, and the listing 10 s later read count=0 |
| xr1, Windows | E1, `TerminateJobObject`, `ActiveProcesses` 0 after 0.31 ms | count=1: the supervisor only. M1 to M4 and M6 ended |
| xr2, Windows | E2 with kill-on-job-close (`LimitFlags=0x2000`), `taskkill /F /PID 36852` | count=0 from a fresh process: M1 to M4 and M6 ended |
| xr3, Windows | E2 without kill-on-job-close (`LimitFlags=0x0000`), `taskkill /F /PID 35400` | M1 to M4 and M6 alive at +12 s and at +22 s. Fresh processes 26888 and 12844 got `NULL` and error 2 from `OpenJobObjectW`. The driver then ended the survivors |

Before each ending, every member was alive and in the spike's job (Windows) or its process group,
session and cgroup (macOS and Linux), as each run's `BEFORE ENDING` listing shows.

## The table

### Native Windows: a Job Object per dispatch, with kill-on-job-close

The next card should do these things:

- Create one job per dispatch from the supervisor's own process, through koffi or an equivalent
  in-process FFI.
- Set `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`, and set no breakaway limit.
- Assign the dispatch's root before it starts children. Node's `spawn` offers no suspended start,
  so this needs a gate like the fixture's, or `CreateProcessW` with `CREATE_SUSPENDED` through the
  FFI.
- End the dispatch with `TerminateJobObject`.

Kill-on-job-close then ends the whole tree when the supervisor is killed outright (x3). That is
stronger than macOS and Linux, where E2 leaves the tree for the next start.

The next card must not assume the following:

- That a non-elevated supervisor can do the same. Only an elevated one was measured.
- That a job without kill-on-job-close can be reached after the supervisor dies. Its name goes with
  the last handle (x4), so a "next start ends the remains" design, as `R-STATE-10` has, does not
  carry over without a second handle holder. That was not measured.
- That the outer job a host puts Rigger in is benign. Under SSH, the session's job ended every member
  on disconnect, which is a containment Rigger did not choose.

**What would reverse it:**

- A non-elevated supervisor that cannot create or assign the job.
- An agent tool that starts processes with `CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB` under a job that allows it.
- Resume across a supervisor restart (the Temporal track) turning out to need dispatches that
  outlive the supervisor, because kill-on-job-close forbids exactly that.

## What this would change, as a proposal

This section is a proposal for the PM and the architect. The report changes none of these.

| Document | What the recommendation would change |
|---|---|
| `D13` | Rule 1 makes macOS v0's only host and bars Windows-specific code before the WSL2 spike reports. A Linux or Windows host with its own containment backend is the deferred row "A native Windows host, with its own containment backend" |
| `R-STATE-17` | Containment is defined as a process group plus a census of the dispatch's directory. A cgroup or a job would be a containment unit that also holds M6, which the definition leaves outside |
| `R-STATE-9`, `R-STATE-10` and `R-STATE-11` | The killed-outright path: the next start ends each recorded process group, and must confirm that the group is still Rigger's. On Linux the recorded thing would be a cgroup path. On Windows, kill-on-job-close would end the tree at once, and nothing would be left for the next start |
| `R-STATE-19` and `R-STATE-26` | What Rigger records when it cannot read a group. A cgroup's cgroup.procs and a job's process list are different reads |
| `ARCHITECTURE.md`, "Failure model" | L0's containment paragraph: "A process that leaves its group is outside this containment" |
| `src/substrate/process.mjs` (`runCommand`) | The code the paragraph describes. This is a pointer for the architect, not a requirement |

## `docs/v0-build-plan.md` §5

This spike ran none of §5's steps. It ran no `wsl --status` or `wsl -l -v`, no rustup, `gh`, Claude
CLI or Tauri libraries, no Emend suite, no `cargo` build, no `claude -p` dispatch, and no Windows
native test comparison.

It did unpack a pinned Node in WSL, which resembles part of step 2, but only as the fixture's
runtime, in the spike's own directory, and removed afterwards. It answers nothing of §5's verdict.

## What failed

- **The first WSL run lost its arguments.** `ssh hostpc [name withheld] -e bash -lc "… e1 w1"` passed
  through cmd.exe, and the runner started with no arguments. It started a supervisor (pid 630)
  that crashed, and a unit `[name withheld]` that failed. I then ended the runner (pid 618). From
  then on, every WSL command went in as a script on stdin.
- **A direct inspection command broke in cmd.exe.** cmd.exe split it at its pipes, and it
  returned `'fx-w1' is not recognized`. It was run outside the transcript wrapper, so the
  transcript's appendix records it.
- **w3's fresh-writer loop never ended.** It polled cgroup.events until `populated 0`, but systemd
  removed the cgroup once it emptied. The loop then spun on `grep` errors (20,209 lines) until I
  ended it (pid 2377). It forked many short processes, which is why WSL's pids jumped from about
  2,400 to 103,000. Its reading stood, because w3's listing at 00:51:55Z read `count=0`. w5 reran
  E2 with a loop that also stops when the cgroup is gone.
- **The first macOS run failed to start.** The worktree's `package.json` sets `"type": "module"`, so
  supervisor.js failed to load. It was renamed to `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/supervisor.cjs`.
- **`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture/list.sh` printed a literal `3N` in its first timestamps**, because macOS `date` has no `%N`. It
  was fixed after m1. m1's listings carry .3NZ.
- **`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/runners/wsl-run.sh` relayed `$$` into a systemd `ExecStart` for w7.** systemd read it as a literal `$`,
  so the line `supervisor-process pid $` carries no pid. The `id` line above it is still the
  supervisor process's own, because `ExecStart` `exec`s into Node. The unit's main pid, 104328, is
  the supervisor's pid in the listing.

- **The first commit was refused.** The transcript was first staged with a Markdown extension, and
  a test requires every tracked Markdown file under `docs/spikes/` to be a checked report. It is
  evidence, not a report, so it was renamed to
  `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/transcript.txt`.
- **Three more pre-commit refusals and one CI failure followed.**
  - Two backticked bare file names in the report failed the path check.
  - A timing test in `test/loop.test.mjs` failed while the laptop's load average was about 100, and
    passed 3 of 3 times on its own.
  - CI's `fast` job then failed on the backticked gitignored spike directory, which a clean checkout
    lacks. `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/ci-fast-136023691075-excerpt.txt` holds that output.
- **Round 2's first `npm install` took a marker argument as a package name** and failed with E404.
  It was rerun without the marker.
- **Round 2's first two WSL file copies wrote wrong bytes.** In the first, `rec` ran `base64` in the
  background without its here-document as stdin. In the second, the redirect of `rec`'s call also
  caught `rec`'s own records. The third copy's sha256 matched the laptop's for all five files, and
  only those copies ran.
- **Round 2's check A stopped the Windows watcher before the watcher had delivered check A's own
  start events.** That left four of check A's processes unrecorded ("Every process the spike
  started").
- **Round 2's first attempt at check B failed to start.** zsh's `echo` read `\c` in a comment as
  "stop output" and merged two lines, so Node raised `ReferenceError: list is not defined`. The
  retry wrote the script with Python.

No single file is a complete transcript. Four files hold the record, and each one's scope is
stated here:

- **`docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/session-log.txt`** holds the tool calls in the spike
  engineer's retained Claude Code session record, as of its generation at 2026-11-20T21:41:59Z: 216 calls with
  results, and a 217th (the call that generated it) with none. For each call it gives the input as
  the record holds it, the UTC times it was issued and returned, the host read from the command
  text, and the result text the record holds. It was generated by
  `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/gen-session-log.py`, and it replaces round 1's
  approximate appendix. It is limited in three ways:
  - Calls 25 and 207: the harness saved the result to a file, and the record holds only a preview
    and the file's path. Call 25's output is the 20,209 repeated `grep` lines, which
    `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/transcript.txt` collapses to a note. Call 207's output is
    the commit hook's suite output, which ended `pass 5965`, `fail 0`.

    record holds only their launch notice. What each one printed went to a file, and later calls

    is in `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/rerun/transcript-r2.txt`. For the rest, the session
    log holds only the parts later calls read.

  edit that quoted the scan into this report. The pattern text carries `sk-ant-`, so the scan
  matches itself.

### Host-configuration changes

The stated-intent comment went up before any install:
[1430#7026629400](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1430#issuecomment-7026629400). It
declared no host-configuration change, and none was made: no package install, firewall rule, WSL or
systemd setting, or registry key. The transient units above are runtime state that vanishes with
the unit, not configuration. Round 2's addendum, [1430#7044905230](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1430#issuecomment-7044905230),
went up before its first step and again declared no configuration change. Round 2's WMI
subscriptions were temporary registrations made with `Register-CimIndicationEvent`, ended with the
watcher, and left no permanent WMI subscription behind. Nothing was reverted, because nothing was
changed. The spike made no WSL restart, no
.wslconfig or `/etc/wsl.conf` change, and no reboot, so no sibling card needed to clear one.
The WSL VM's own stops between round 2's sessions came from its idle timeout, not from any command
of the spike.

## Questions this leaves open, and what would settle them

| Open question | What would settle it |
|---|---|
| Can a non-elevated Windows supervisor create and assign a job? | Run `docs/spikes/whole-tree-containment/fixture-win/` from a standard user's SSH session. That needs a non-admin account on the Windows machine, which is a host-configuration change for the owner to approve |
| Does the systemd user manager, and so the delegated cgroup, exist without an open login session? | Rerun w1 with every session closed and `loginctl enable-linger` unset. Enabling linger would be a configuration change |
| Does a second handle holder keep an orphaned Windows job reachable without kill-on-job-close? | One run with a holder process that opens the named job and outlives the supervisor |
| Does a real agent session stay in its cgroup or job? | That is outside this card, by its scope edges |

**Hosts, and whose custody each is under.**

- **The WSL2 machine and the Windows machine are the primary hosts.** Heavy or long runs go there. The spike reaches each over SSH from the laptop ([relay](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1429#issuecomment-7014074437), answer 4), at these hosts:
  - the WSL2 machine: `wsl-hostpc` (`user@10.0.0.29`, port 2222, a Windows port proxy into WSL's sshd; until that sshd answers, WSL is reached through the Windows host with `ssh hostpc [name withheld] -e …`);
  - the Windows machine: `hostpc` (`user@10.0.0.29`, port 2223, Windows OpenSSH).
- **The laptop** (macOS) is enrolled under M8's custody rule ([M8-U45](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1293#issuecomment-6980322109), [M8-U58](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1293#issuecomment-6988027619)). It takes light macOS runs only, and none of these:
  - reading, writing or running anything in `~/GitHub/emend-engine`, `~/GitHub/emend-engine-worktrees`, `~/GitHub/emend` or `~/rigger-builds/`, on Emend's board (project 13), or in the williacj/emend repository;
  - a LaunchAgent with the `[name withheld]` prefix, or anything in `~/Library/LaunchAgents/[name withheld]` or `~/Library/Logs/rigger/`;
  - a Rigger run from a checkout, or one against Emend (`R-SAFE-5`);
  - a git GUI, or an IDE that fetches by itself;
  - a long CPU-heavy job during an M8 canary or window period, which rigger-ae announces.
- **The Mac mini** is under the M8 coordinator's custody and is offline. Before any use, the spike tells rigger-ae on this card and waits for rigger-ae to relay the M8 coordinator's OK. Every command there runs through `~/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/coordinator-tools/suite-slot.sh <tag> -- …`. The spike never touches the `judge-*` or `codex-*` clones there.

**Rules on how it runs.**

- **Where:** throwaway work lives in a directory named `<card>-spike-engineer`, where `<card>` is this issue's number: under the gitignored `spikes/` directory on a host with a Rigger checkout, and under a scratch directory of the spike's own making on any other host. It never merges (`AGENTS.md`, "When you write code"). Nothing runs Rigger from a checkout (`R-SAFE-5`).
- **Transcript:** the spike keeps a full transcript of every command it runs, each with its UTC time and host, and discloses every write it did not plan, with its reason (owner ruling [P32](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/642#issuecomment-6089559859)).
- **Stated intent:** before its first install or host-configuration change on any host, the spike posts a comment on this card listing each one it intends, with the host. A configuration change is anything outside its own directories that persists: a package install, a firewall rule, a WSL or systemd setting, a registry key.
- **Evidence:** every file the acceptance cites by path, except where an item says otherwise, is committed in the report's pull request under `docs/spikes/<report>/`, beside the report, so a judge opens it at the judged head. The transcript is among them.
- **Sibling spikes share the WSL2 machine.** The three Temporal-track spikes may run there at once. Before a WSL restart, a `.wslconfig` or `/etc/wsl.conf` change, or any other step that ends processes it did not start, a spike posts the step on all three cards and waits for each sibling spike, or rigger-ae, to answer that none of its recorded processes would be ended. #1429's Temporal server uses the default ports, 7233 and 8233. #1431's uses 7243 for gRPC and 8243 for the UI.
- **Processes:** the spike records the pid of every process it starts, and ends only those, or processes descended from them (`AGENTS.md`, "End only recorded processes").
- **Claude Code:** any live `claude` run uses a `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` the spike made, never the owner's `~/.claude`.
- **M8 and the live board:** the spike writes nothing to the live Rigger board (project 6), to any card in the M8 milestone, or to any branch but this card's.
- **Temporal:** the local development server (`temporal server start-dev`) is the server for this track. Its docs say it is not intended for production use; this spike measures behaviour, not production fitness.

// ABOUTME: Card #1430 Windows helpers: koffi bindings to kernel32/ntdll Job Object and process calls, and the listing.
'use strict';
const koffi = require('koffi');
const { spawnSync } = require('node:child_process');
const k = koffi.load('kernel32.dll');
const nt = koffi.load('ntdll.dll');
const W = {
  CreateJobObjectW: k.func('void* __stdcall CreateJobObjectW(void*, str16)'),
  OpenJobObjectW: k.func('void* __stdcall OpenJobObjectW(uint32, int, str16)'),
  SetInformationJobObject: k.func('int __stdcall SetInformationJobObject(void*, int, void*, uint32)'),
  QueryInformationJobObject: k.func('int __stdcall QueryInformationJobObject(void*, int, void*, uint32, void*)'),
  AssignProcessToJobObject: k.func('int __stdcall AssignProcessToJobObject(void*, void*)'),
  TerminateJobObject: k.func('int __stdcall TerminateJobObject(void*, uint32)'),
  IsProcessInJob: k.func('int __stdcall IsProcessInJob(void*, void*, _Out_ int*)'),
  OpenProcess: k.func('void* __stdcall OpenProcess(uint32, int, uint32)'),
  CloseHandle: k.func('int __stdcall CloseHandle(void*)'),
  GetCurrentProcess: k.func('void* __stdcall GetCurrentProcess()'),
  GetLastError: k.func('uint32 __stdcall GetLastError()'),
  ReadProcessMemory: k.func('int __stdcall ReadProcessMemory(void*, uint64, void*, uint64, void*)'),
  NtQueryInformationProcess: nt.func('int32 __stdcall NtQueryInformationProcess(void*, int, void*, uint32, void*)'),
};
const KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE = 0x2000;
const jobName = (run) => `Local\\fx1430-${run}`;

function limits(job) {
  const b = Buffer.alloc(144);
  if (!W.QueryInformationJobObject(job, 9, b, 144, null)) return `QueryInformationJobObject(9) failed, error ${W.GetLastError()}`;
  const f = b.readUInt32LE(16);
  return `JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION.BasicLimitInformation.LimitFlags=0x${f.toString(16).padStart(4, '0')} KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE=${(f & KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE) ? 'set' : 'not set'}`;
}
function setKillOnClose(job) {
  const b = Buffer.alloc(144); b.writeUInt32LE(KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE, 16);
  return W.SetInformationJobObject(job, 9, b, 144);
}
function active(job) {
  const b = Buffer.alloc(48);
  if (!W.QueryInformationJobObject(job, 1, b, 48, null)) return null;
  return { total: b.readUInt32LE(36), active: b.readUInt32LE(40), terminated: b.readUInt32LE(44) };
}
function pidsIn(job) {
  const b = Buffer.alloc(8 + 8 * 512);
  if (!W.QueryInformationJobObject(job, 3, b, b.length, null)) return `failed, error ${W.GetLastError()}`;
  const n = b.readUInt32LE(4); const out = [];
  for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) out.push(Number(b.readBigUInt64LE(8 + 8 * i)));
  return out;
}
function inJob(pid, job) {
  const h = W.OpenProcess(0x1000, 0, pid);
  if (!h) return `OpenProcess error ${W.GetLastError()}`;
  const r = [0]; const ok = W.IsProcessInJob(h, job, r); const e = W.GetLastError(); W.CloseHandle(h);
  return ok ? (r[0] ? 'yes' : 'no') : `IsProcessInJob error ${e}`;
}
function cwdOf(pid) {
  const h = W.OpenProcess(0x0410, 0, pid);
  if (!h) return `?(OpenProcess error ${W.GetLastError()})`;
  try {
    const pbi = Buffer.alloc(48);
    if (W.NtQueryInformationProcess(h, 0, pbi, 48, null) !== 0) return '?(NtQueryInformationProcess)';
    const peb = pbi.readBigUInt64LE(8);
    const p = Buffer.alloc(8); if (!W.ReadProcessMemory(h, peb + 0x20n, p, 8n, null)) return '?(read PEB)';
    const params = p.readBigUInt64LE(0);
    const us = Buffer.alloc(16); if (!W.ReadProcessMemory(h, params + 0x38n, us, 16n, null)) return '?(read params)';
    const len = us.readUInt16LE(0); const buf = us.readBigUInt64LE(8);
    const s = Buffer.alloc(len); if (!W.ReadProcessMemory(h, buf, s, BigInt(len), null)) return '?(read string)';
    return s.toString('utf16le');
  } finally { W.CloseHandle(h); }
}
function processes(run) {
  const ps = `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "CommandLine like '%fx1430-${run}-%'" | Select-Object ProcessId,ParentProcessId,SessionId,CommandLine | ConvertTo-Json -Compress`;
  const out = spawnSync('powershell.exe', ['-NoProfile', '-NonInteractive', '-Command', ps], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout.trim();
  if (!out) return [];
  const j = JSON.parse(out); return (Array.isArray(j) ? j : [j]).filter((p) => !/Get-CimInstance/.test(p.CommandLine));
}
// job: a handle to the spike's job, or null (then only "in any job" is shown).
function list(run, job) {
  const rows = processes(run);
  const lines = [`listing run=${run} at ${new Date().toISOString()} on Windows (Win32_Process + IsProcessInJob + PEB CurrentDirectory)`,
    'PID     PPID    SESS  IN_SPIKE_JOB IN_ANY_JOB CWD | ROLE'];
  for (const r of rows) {
    const role = (r.CommandLine.match(/fx1430-[a-z0-9]+-(\w+)/) || [])[1];
    lines.push(`${String(r.ProcessId).padEnd(7)} ${String(r.ParentProcessId).padEnd(7)} ${String(r.SessionId).padEnd(5)} ${(job ? inJob(r.ProcessId, job) : 'n/a').padEnd(12)} ${inJob(r.ProcessId, null).padEnd(10)} ${cwdOf(r.ProcessId)} | ${role}`);
  }
  lines.push(`count=${rows.length}`);
  return lines.join('\n');
}
// Image name and parent of each pid, for annotating a job's process id list.
function names(pids) {
  if (!Array.isArray(pids) || !pids.length) return pids;
  const ps = `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "${pids.map((p) => `ProcessId=${p}`).join(' OR ')}" | Select-Object ProcessId,ParentProcessId,Name | ConvertTo-Json -Compress`;
  const out = spawnSync('powershell.exe', ['-NoProfile', '-NonInteractive', '-Command', ps], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout.trim();
  const j = out ? [].concat(JSON.parse(out)) : [];
  return pids.map((p) => { const r = j.find((x) => x.ProcessId === p); return r ? `${p}:${r.Name}(ppid ${r.ParentProcessId})` : `${p}:gone`; });
}
module.exports = { names, W, KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE, jobName, limits, setKillOnClose, active, pidsIn, inJob, cwdOf, list };

=== rerun/rec.cjs
// ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun: records every process this Node process starts (pid, command, times, exit) as JSON lines in $FX_REC.
'use strict';
const cp = require('node:child_process');
const fs = require('node:fs');
const os = require('node:os');
const FILE = process.env.FX_REC;
const HOST = process.env.FX_HOST || os.hostname();
function note(o) {
  const line = JSON.stringify({ t: new Date().toISOString(), host: HOST, by: process.pid, ...o });
  if (FILE) fs.appendFileSync(FILE, line + '\n');
  return line;
}
note({ event: 'self', pid: process.pid, ppid: process.ppid, argv: process.argv });
function spawnSync(cmd, args, opts) {
  const started = new Date().toISOString();
  const r = cp.spawnSync(cmd, args, opts);
  note({ event: 'spawnSync', started, pid: r.pid, cmd, args, status: r.status, signal: r.signal });
  return r;
}
function spawn(cmd, args, opts) {
  const c = cp.spawn(cmd, args, opts);
  note({ event: 'spawn', pid: c.pid, cmd, args });
  c.on('exit', (code, sig) => note({ event: 'exit', pid: c.pid, code, sig }));
  return c;
}
module.exports = { spawnSync, spawn, note };
=== rerun/orch.cjs
// ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun step runner on the laptop: runs one command (optionally fed a script on stdin), records its pid through
// rec.cjs, and appends the exact argv, stdin script, UTC start and end, host, exit and full output to transcript-r2.txt.
// Usage: FX_REC=... node orch.cjs <host-label> <stdin-file|-> -- <cmd> [args...]
'use strict';
const fs = require('node:fs');
const path = require('node:path');
const crypto = require('node:crypto');
const rec = require('./rec.cjs');
const [host, input, sep, cmd, ...args] = process.argv.slice(2);
if (sep !== '--') throw new Error('usage');
const T = path.join(__dirname, 'transcript-r2.txt');
const start = new Date().toISOString();
const stdin = input === '-' ? null : fs.readFileSync(input);
const c = rec.spawn(cmd, args, { stdio: [stdin ? 'pipe' : 'ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'], env: process.env });
if (stdin) { c.stdin.end(stdin); }
let out = '';
c.stdout.on('data', (d) => { out += d; process.stdout.write(d); });
c.stderr.on('data', (d) => { out += d; process.stderr.write(d); });
c.on('close', (code, sig) => {
  const end = new Date().toISOString();
  const head = [`### ${start} -> ${end}  host=${host}  orch pid ${process.pid} (ppid ${process.ppid}), child pid ${c.pid}, exit ${code}${sig ? ' signal ' + sig : ''}`,
    '$ ' + JSON.stringify([cmd, ...args])];
  if (stdin) head.push(`stdin: ${input} (sha256 ${crypto.createHash('sha256').update(stdin).digest('hex')}):`, '<<<', stdin.toString().replace(/\n$/, ''), '>>>');
  fs.appendFileSync(T, '\n' + head.join('\n') + '\n--- output ---\n' + out.replace(/\n$/, '') + '\n');
  process.exit(code === null ? 1 : code);
});
=== rerun/watcher.ps1
# ABOUTME: Card #1430 rerun Windows watcher (fx1430r2): logs every process start and stop on the host, from WMI's temporary
# Win32_ProcessStartTrace and Win32_ProcessStopTrace subscriptions, until C:\Users\user\1430-r2\stop exists.
$dir = 'C:\Users\user\1430-r2'
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force $dir | Out-Null
$log = "$dir\watch.log"
function W($s) { Add-Content -Path $log -Value $s -Encoding utf8 }
function Now { (Get-Date).ToUniversalTime().ToString('o') }
$p = $PID
for ($i = 0; $i -lt 6 -and $p; $i++) {
  $a = Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "ProcessId=$p"
  if (-not $a) { break }
  W "chain $(Now) pid=$($a.ProcessId) ppid=$($a.ParentProcessId) name=$($a.Name) created=$($a.CreationDate.ToUniversalTime().ToString('o')) cmd=$($a.CommandLine)"
  $p = $a.ParentProcessId
}
Register-CimIndicationEvent -ClassName Win32_ProcessStartTrace -SourceIdentifier s | Out-Null
Register-CimIndicationEvent -ClassName Win32_ProcessStopTrace -SourceIdentifier e | Out-Null
W "ready $(Now) watcher pid=$PID"
"watcher ready pid=$PID"
while (-not (Test-Path "$dir\stop")) {
  $ev = Wait-Event -Timeout 2
  if ($ev) {
    $n = $ev.SourceEventArgs.NewEvent
    $t = [DateTime]::FromFileTimeUtc([long]$n.TIME_CREATED).ToString('o')
    if ($ev.SourceIdentifier -eq 's') {
      $c = (Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "ProcessId=$($n.ProcessID)" -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue).CommandLine
      W "start $t pid=$($n.ProcessID) ppid=$($n.ParentProcessID) name=$($n.ProcessName) cmd=$c"
    } else { W "stop $t pid=$($n.ProcessID) ppid=$($n.ParentProcessID) name=$($n.ProcessName)" }
    Remove-Event -EventIdentifier $ev.EventIdentifier
  }
}
Unregister-Event -SourceIdentifier s; Unregister-Event -SourceIdentifier e
W "stopped $(Now)"
"watcher stopped"

# ABOUTME: Card #1430: turns the spike engineer's retained Claude Code session record (JSON lines) into a plain-text log of every tool
# call, with its UTC issue and completion times, the host it acted on, its exact input and its full result, redacting one account name.
import json, re, sys
src, dst = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
uses, results, order = {}, {}, []
for line in open(src):
    d = json.loads(line)
    if d.get('type') not in ('assistant', 'user') or not d.get('timestamp'):
        continue
    c = d.get('message', {}).get('content')
    if not isinstance(c, list):
        continue
    for x in c:
        if x.get('type') == 'tool_use' and x['id'] not in uses:
            uses[x['id']] = (d['timestamp'], x['name'], x['input']); order.append(x['id'])
        if x.get('type') == 'tool_result':
            body = x.get('content')
            if isinstance(body, list):
                body = '\n'.join(b.get('text', '[non-text block]') if isinstance(b, dict) else str(b) for b in body)
            results[x['tool_use_id']] = (d['timestamp'], bool(x.get('is_error')), body if body is not None else '')
def host(name, inp):
    if name != 'Bash':
        return 'laptop'
    cmd = inp.get('command', '')
    if '[name withheld]' in cmd or 'wsl-hostpc' in cmd: return 'laptop -> hostpc -> WSL'
    if 'hostpc' in cmd: return 'laptop -> hostpc'
    return 'laptop'
def redact(s):
    s = re.sub(r'MicrosoftAccount\\\S+(\s+)User(\s+)S-1-11-96-[0-9-]+', lambda m: 'MicrosoftAccount\\[redacted: account name]' + m.group(1) + 'User' + m.group(2) + 'S-1-11-96-[redacted]', s)
    s = re.sub(r'[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+@[name withheld]\.com', '[redacted: account name]', s)
    return s
out = ['ABOUTME: The tool calls in the #1430 spike engineer\'s retained Claude Code session record, with UTC times, host, input and the result text the record holds.', '',
       'Generated by gen-session-log.py from the session record of subagent a9a3ffa429348b341. Times are UTC: the first is when the call was issued, the second when its result returned.',
       '"host" is read from the command text: laptop unless the command reaches hostpc over ssh, and WSL when it runs [name withheld]. A command that ran in the background shows its launch result here; its output file is named in the result.',
       'Redactions: one Microsoft account name and its SID, wherever whoami /groups output appears. Nothing else is removed. A call that returned an error is marked ERROR.',
       'Scope: only calls the record holds up to the time it was generated. A result the harness saved to a file holds only a preview here, and a background command holds only its launch notice. The report, under "The transcript", lists those calls and what the log lacks.', '']
for n, i in enumerate(order, 1):
    t0, name, inp = uses[i]
    t1, err, body = results.get(i, (None, False, '[no result recorded: the session record ends before it]'))
    shown = inp.get('command') if name == 'Bash' and set(inp) <= {'command', 'description', 'timeout', 'run_in_background', 'dangerouslyDisableSandbox'} else json.dumps(inp, ensure_ascii=False, indent=1)
    extra = '' if name != 'Bash' else '  ' + json.dumps({k: v for k, v in inp.items() if k != 'command'}, ensure_ascii=False)
    out.append(f'### {n}. {t0} -> {t1}  host={host(name, inp)}  tool={name}{"  ERROR" if err else ""}{extra}')
    out.append(redact(shown if isinstance(shown, str) else str(shown)))
    out.append('--- result ---')
    out.append(redact(str(body)))
    out.append('')
open(dst, 'w').write('\n'.join(out))
print(len(order), 'calls')
OpenSSH_10.5p1, LibreSSL 3.3.6

'use strict';
const { spawn } = require('./rec.cjs');
const path = require('node:path');
const run = process.argv[2].replace(/^fx1430-|-root$/g, '');
const member = path.join(__dirname, 'member-win.cjs');
const tag = (m) => `fx1430-${run}-${m}`;
process.stdin.once('data', () => {
  process.stdin.destroy();
  // M1: a direct child; it starts M2, a grandchild.
  spawn(process.execPath, [member, tag('M1'), 'M1'], { stdio: 'ignore' });
  // M3: a descendant started detached, in a new process group (libuv: DETACHED_PROCESS | CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP).
  spawn(process.execPath, [member, tag('M3'), 'leaf'], { stdio: 'ignore', detached: true }).unref();
  // M4: a grandchild whose parent (cmd.exe running `start /b`) exits at once.
  spawn('cmd.exe', [`/d /c start "" /b "${process.execPath}" "${member}" ${tag('M4')} leaf`], { stdio: 'ignore', windowsVerbatimArguments: true });

  spawn(process.execPath, [member, tag('M6'), 'M6'], { stdio: 'ignore', detached: true }).unref();
  setTimeout(() => {}, 300000);
});

// ABOUTME: Card #1458 process record on Windows: appends one JSON line per process (host, pid, creation time from
// GetProcessTimes, the UTC time written, who wrote it and how) to the file named by $FX_REC, at the moment it starts.
'use strict';
const cp = require('node:child_process');
const fs = require('node:fs');
const w = require('./win.cjs');
function note(o) {
  const line = JSON.stringify({ host: 'hostpc', ...o, recorded: new Date().toISOString(), by: process.pid });
  if (process.env.FX_REC) fs.appendFileSync(process.env.FX_REC, line + '\n');
  return line;
}
function entry(pid, how, what) { const c = w.created(pid); return note({ pid, created: c.iso || null, createdFt: c.ft, createdErr: c.err, how, what }); }
function self(what) { return entry(process.pid, 'self', what || process.argv.slice(1).join(' ')); }
function spawn(cmd, args, opts) {
  const c = cp.spawn(cmd, args, opts);
  entry(c.pid, 'spawn', [cmd, ...args].join(' '));
  return c;
}
module.exports = { note, entry, self, spawn };
EOF

const path = require('node:path');
const run = process.argv[2].replace(/^fx1458-|-root$/g, '');
const member = path.join(__dirname, 'member.cjs');
const tag = (m) => `fx1458-${run}-${m}`;
rec.self();
process.stdin.once('data', () => {
  process.stdin.destroy();
  // M1: a direct child; it starts M2, a grandchild.
  rec.spawn(process.execPath, [member, tag('M1'), 'M1'], { stdio: 'ignore' });
  // M3: a descendant started detached, in a new process group (libuv: DETACHED_PROCESS | CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP).
  rec.spawn(process.execPath, [member, tag('M3'), 'leaf'], { stdio: 'ignore', detached: true }).unref();
  // M4: a grandchild whose parent (cmd.exe running `start /b`) exits at once.
  rec.spawn(process.env.ComSpec || 'C:\\Windows\\System32\\cmd.exe', [`/d /c start "" /b "${process.execPath}" "${member}" ${tag('M4')} leaf`], { stdio: 'ignore', windowsVerbatimArguments: true });
  // M6: a descendant started detached that changes its working directory to the drive root.
  rec.spawn(process.execPath, [member, tag('M6'), 'M6'], { stdio: 'ignore', detached: true }).unref();
  setTimeout(() => {}, 300000);
});

// ABOUTME: Card #1458 fixture member: node member.cjs <tag> <role>; records itself, starts M2 for role M1, moves to the

    log(`supervisor ${sup} exited with code ${exited()}`);

    while (!/waiting to be killed|stopping/.test(supLog())) { if (exited() !== null) break; await sleep(100); }
    log('launcher ancestry before the kill:', chain());
    log('IMMEDIATELY BEFORE ENDING\n' + w.list(`run=${run} immediately before taskkill`, tag, null).text);
    const k = cp.spawnSync('taskkill', ['/F', '/PID', String(sup)], { encoding: 'utf8' });
    log(`taskkill /F /PID ${sup} (taskkill pid ${k.pid}, exit ${k.status}):`, (k.stdout + k.stderr).trim());
    await sleep(12000);
    log('launcher ancestry at the post-kill listing:', chain());
    log('AFTER ENDING (+12 s after taskkill)\n' + w.list(`run=${run} 12 s after taskkill`, tag, null).text);
    log('launcher ancestry after the post-kill listing:', chain());
  }
  track(); clearInterval(timer);
  const live = w.snapshot();
  for (const [p, ft] of tree) {
    if (!live.find((x) => x.pid === p) || w.created(p).ft !== ft) continue;
    const h = w.W.OpenProcess(0x0001, 0, p);
    log(`ending survivor ${p} (recorded, created ${w.ftIso(ft)}):`, h && w.W.TerminateProcess(h, 1) ? 'ok' : `failed ${w.W.GetLastError()}`);
  }
  await sleep(1000);
  log('AFTER CLEANUP\n' + w.list(`run=${run} after cleanup`, tag, null).text);
  console.log(`---- sup.log (${run})\n` + supLog());
  for (const f of ['rec-user.jsonl', 'rec-fx1458.jsonl']) { let s = ''; try { s = fs.readFileSync(path.join(rundir, f), 'utf8'); } catch (er) { s = String(er.code); } console.log(`---- ${f} (${run})\n` + s.trimEnd()); }
  log('launcher done');
})();
EOF

### 30. 2026-11-22T03:05:17.042Z -> 2026-11-22T03:05:17.232Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Fix how the supervisor reads koffi's version"}
cd /Users/cjwilliams/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1458-spike-engineer/spikes/1458-spike-engineer/win && python3 - <<'EOF'
p='supervisor.cjs'; s=open(p).read()
old="""const ver = (p) => { try { return require(p).version; } catch (e) { return `not loaded (${e.code})`; } };"""
new="""// koffi's package.json is not in its exports map, so each package.json is read from the directory its entry point resolves in.
const ver = (p) => { try { return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(path.dirname(require.resolve(p)), 'package.json'), 'utf8')).version; } catch (e) { return `not read (${e.code})`; } };"""
assert old in s; s=s.replace(old,new)
s=s.replace("""'| koffi', ver('koffi/package.json'), '| @koromix/koffi-win32-x64', ver('@koromix/koffi-win32-x64/package.json'));""","""'| koffi', ver('koffi'), '| @koromix/koffi-win32-x64', ver('@koromix/koffi-win32-x64/package.json'), '| koffi.version', require('koffi').version);""")
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF
grep -n "koffi.version" supervisor.cjs

### 31. 2026-11-22T03:05:25.493Z -> 2026-11-22T03:05:44.923Z  host=laptop -> hostpc  tool=Bash  {"description": "Copy the supervisor and run n4 (E1 repeat)", "timeout": 120000}
cd /Users/cjwilliams/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1458-spike-engineer/spikes/1458-spike-engineer/win && python3 - <<'EOF'
p='supervisor.cjs'; s=open(p).read()
old="""const ver = (p) => { try { return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(path.join(path.dirname(require.resolve(p)), 'package.json'), 'utf8')).version; } catch (e) { return `not read (${e.code})`; } };"""
new="""const ver = (p) => {
  try {
    for (let d = path.dirname(require.resolve(p)); d !== path.dirname(d); d = path.dirname(d)) {
      const f = path.join(d, 'package.json');
      if (fs.existsSync(f)) { const j = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8')); if (j.name && p.startsWith(j.name)) return `${j.version} (${f})`; }
    }
    return 'no package.json found';
  } catch (e) { return `not read (${e.code})`; }
};"""
assert old in s; s=s.replace(old,new)
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF

p='launcher.cjs'; s=open(p).read()
old="""    log(`taskkill /F /PID ${sup} (taskkill pid ${k.pid}, exit ${k.status}):`, (k.stdout + k.stderr).trim());
    await sleep(12000);"""
new="""    const tk = process.hrtime.bigint();
    log(`taskkill /F /PID ${sup} (taskkill pid ${k.pid}, exit ${k.status}):`, (k.stdout + k.stderr).trim());
    // From n6: how soon after taskkill returned the recorded tree (the supervisor excepted) had no live process, polled every 5 ms for 2 s.
    const others = [...tree].filter(([p]) => p !== sup);
    const liveOf = () => { const s = new Set(w.snapshot().map((x) => x.pid)); return others.filter(([p, ft]) => s.has(p) && w.created(p).ft === ft).map(([p]) => p); };
    let gone = null; let last = liveOf();
    while (Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - tk) / 1e6 < 2000) { last = liveOf(); if (!last.length) { gone = Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - tk) / 1e6; break; } await sleep(5); }
    log(gone === null ? `recorded tree still had live processes 2 s after taskkill returned: ${last.join(',')}` : `none of the ${others.length} recorded tree processes (supervisor excepted) alive ${gone.toFixed(1)} ms after taskkill returned (first poll that found none)`);
    await sleep(12000 - Number(process.hrtime.bigint() - tk) / 1e6);"""
assert old in s; s=s.replace(old,new)
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF

### 34. 2026-11-22T03:07:15.395Z -> 2026-11-22T03:07:16.846Z  host=laptop -> hostpc  tool=Bash  {"description": "Sweep Windows processes and check for a profile before deletion", "timeout": 180000}
cd /Users/cjwilliams/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1458-spike-engineer/spikes/1458-spike-engineer/win && cat > sweep.cjs <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Card #1458 final sweep on Windows, run as user: lists every process on the host with its pid and creation time,
// and reports any that matches a process-record entry on both, and any that runs as the fx1458 account.
// Usage: node sweep.cjs <record.jsonl> [more.jsonl...]
'use strict';
const fs = require('node:fs');
const w = require('./win.cjs');
const FX_SID = '[SID withheld]';
console.log(new Date().toISOString(), 'sweep pid', process.pid, w.enableDebug());
const entries = [];
for (const f of process.argv.slice(2)) for (const l of fs.readFileSync(f, 'utf8').split('\n')) if (l.trim()) entries.push(JSON.parse(l));
const keys = new Set(entries.filter((e) => e.createdFt).map((e) => `${e.pid}:${e.createdFt}`));
console.log(`record entries read: ${entries.length}, distinct (pid, creation time) pairs: ${keys.size}, from ${process.argv.length - 2} files`);
function ownerSid(pid) {
  const h = w.W.OpenProcess(0x1000, 0, pid); if (!h) return null;
  try {
    const t = [null]; if (!w.W.OpenProcessToken(h, 0x0008, t)) return null;
    const b = Buffer.alloc(256); const n = [0]; const ok = w.W.GetTokenInformation(t[0], 1, b, 256, n); w.W.CloseHandle(t[0]);
    if (!ok) return null; const s = b.subarray(16); const subs = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < s[1]; i++) subs.push(s.readUInt32LE(8 + 4 * i));
    let auth = 0; for (let i = 2; i < 8; i++) auth = auth * 256 + s[i];
    return `S-${s[0]}-${auth}-${subs.join('-')}`;
  } finally { w.W.CloseHandle(h); }
}
const at = new Date().toISOString();
const rows = w.snapshot().map((p) => ({ ...p, c: w.created(p.pid) }));
console.log(`listing of every process at ${at} (Toolhelp snapshot + GetProcessTimes), ${rows.length} processes`);
console.log('PID     CREATED (UTC, GetProcessTimes)    NAME');
let match = 0; let fx = 0;
for (const r of rows) {
  const m = r.c.ft && keys.has(`${r.pid}:${r.c.ft}`); if (m) match++;
  const o = ownerSid(r.pid) === FX_SID; if (o) fx++;
  console.log(`${String(r.pid).padEnd(7)} ${String(r.c.iso || r.c.err).padEnd(33)} ${r.name}${m ? '  <-- MATCHES A RECORD ENTRY' : ''}${o ? '  <-- RUNS AS fx1458' : ''}`);
}
const pidOnly = rows.filter((r) => entries.some((e) => e.pid === r.pid)).map((r) => r.pid);
console.log(`processes matching a record entry on both pid and creation time: ${match}`);
console.log(`processes running as fx1458 (${FX_SID}): ${fx}`);
console.log(`pids that equal a recorded pid but differ in creation time (reused pids, not matches): ${pidOnly.length ? pidOnly.join(',') : 'none'}`);
EOF
node -e "new (require('vm').Script)(require('fs').readFileSync('sweep.cjs','utf8'))" && cd .. && cat > ps-before-delete.ps1 <<'EOF'
$ProgressPreference = 'SilentlyContinue'
"utc $((Get-Date).ToUniversalTime().ToString('o'))"
Set-Location C:\Users\user\1458-spike-engineer
$recs = Get-ChildItem runs\*\rec-*.jsonl | ForEach-Object FullName
"record files:"; $recs
node sweep.cjs @recs
"profile for fx1458 (Win32_UserProfile with its SID):"
$sid = '[SID withheld]'
$prof = Get-CimInstance Win32_UserProfile -Filter "SID='$sid'"
if ($prof) { $prof | Select-Object SID, LocalPath, Loaded | Format-List | Out-String } else { "  none" }
"ProfileList registry key for that SID exists: $(Test-Path "HKLM:\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows NT\CurrentVersion\ProfileList\$sid")"
"Test-Path C:\Users\fx1458: $(Test-Path C:\Users\fx1458)"
"directories under C:\Users whose name starts fx1458: $((Get-ChildItem C:\Users -Directory -Filter 'fx1458*' | ForEach-Object Name) -join ',')"
"HKU hive loaded for that SID: $(Test-Path "Registry::HKEY_USERS\$sid")"
"done $((Get-Date).ToUniversalTime().ToString('o'))"
EOF

### 37. 2026-11-22T03:07:58.752Z -> 2026-11-22T03:07:59.094Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Final laptop sweep against the laptop process record"}
cd /Users/cjwilliams/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1458-spike-engineer/spikes/1458-spike-engineer && cat > laptop-sweep.cjs <<'EOF'
// ABOUTME: Card #1458 final sweep on the laptop: lists every process with its pid and start time (ps lstart) and reports any
// that matches a laptop process-record entry on both. It excludes itself, whose own entry the step runner wrote as it started it.
'use strict';
const fs = require('node:fs');
const cp = require('node:child_process');
const rec = fs.readFileSync(process.argv[2], 'utf8').trim().split('\n').map((l) => JSON.parse(l));
const keys = new Set(rec.map((e) => `${e.pid}:${e.created}`));
console.log(new Date().toISOString(), `sweep pid ${process.pid}; laptop record entries: ${rec.length}`);
const out = cp.spawnSync('ps', ['-A', '-o', 'pid=,lstart=,comm='], { encoding: 'utf8' }).stdout.trim().split('\n');
console.log(`listing of every process (ps -A -o pid=,lstart=,comm=), ${out.length} processes`);
let match = 0;
for (const l of out) {
  const m = l.trim().match(/^(\d+)\s+(\w{3} \w{3} +\d+ \d\d:\d\d:\d\d \d{4})\s+(.*)$/); if (!m) { console.log('unparsed:', l); continue; }
  const self = Number(m[1]) === process.pid;
  const hit = !self && keys.has(`${m[1]}:${m[2]}`); if (hit) match++;
  console.log(`${m[1].padEnd(7)} ${m[2]}  ${m[3]}${hit ? '  <-- MATCHES A RECORD ENTRY' : ''}${self ? '  (this sweep; excluded)' : ''}`);
}
console.log(`processes matching a laptop record entry on both pid and start time: ${match}`);
EOF
node t.cjs 'laptop' -- node laptop-sweep.cjs ../../docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/process-record-laptop.jsonl | tail -3

### 43. 2026-11-22T03:09:14.566Z -> 2026-11-22T03:09:15.406Z  host=laptop -> hostpc  tool=Bash  {"description": "Identify the npm cache write on Windows", "timeout": 120000}

$ProgressPreference = 'SilentlyContinue'
"utc $((Get-Date).ToUniversalTime().ToString('o'))"
$since = [DateTime]::Parse('2026-11-22T02:57:00Z').ToUniversalTime()
$p = "$env:LOCALAPPDATA\npm-cache"
"$p itself: LastWriteTimeUtc $((Get-Item $p -Force).LastWriteTimeUtc.ToString('o'))"
