--- result ---
ABOUTME: Journal for #1430: whether cgroup v2 under WSL2 and Job Objects on Windows end an agent's whole
process tree, against the macOS baseline and today's route.

# #1430 — Whole-tree containment on Linux under WSL2 and on Windows

## What ran

On 2026-11-20 a throwaway fixture ran on three hosts: macOS on the laptop, Ubuntu 26.04 under WSL2,
and native Windows. The fixture was six sleeping members, two of which left the session and one of
which moved to `/`. Each host ran it under each ending the card names. The code stayed in the
gitignored `spikes/`. Its source, every listing and the transcript are in
[the report](../spikes/whole-tree-containment.md).

## What we learned

- **A cgroup cost nothing on this WSL2 host.** systemd was pid 1, and the user manager handed an
  unprivileged user a writable cgroup through `systemd-run --user -p Delegate=yes`. `cgroup.kill`
  ended every member in 1.66 ms. An orphaned cgroup stayed reachable by its path after the
  supervisor died.
- **The shell `[name withheld] -e` starts is outside systemd's tree.** It sits in `/non-systemd`, owned by
  root. A delegated cgroup needs the user manager to start the process, or a scope to move it.
- **A Windows job's name dies with its last handle, not with the job.** Without kill-on-job-close,
  the members outlived the supervisor in a job that no fresh process could open.
  `OpenJobObjectW` failed with error 2.
- **Today's route misses a process that leaves the session and the dispatch directory together.**
  M6 survived the group kill and the census on macOS and on Linux alike.
- **The SSH session is itself a job on Windows,** and it ended every survivor on disconnect.
  Windows readings taken over SSH must finish inside one session.
- **systemd's default `KillMode` sends `SIGTERM`,** which the member that ignores it survived.

## What failed

- **`cmd.exe` ate the arguments of the first WSL run,** and broke one inspection command at its
  pipes. After that, every WSL command went in as a script on stdin.
- **A polling loop waited for `populated 0` from a cgroup that systemd had removed,** and spun until
  I ended it. WSL's pid counter advanced by about 101,000 while it ran (2377 to 103714). The rerun's loop also stops when the
  cgroup is gone.
- **`supervisor.js` would not load,** because the repository's `package.json` sets
  `"type": "module"`. Throwaway CommonJS in `spikes/` needs the `.cjs` extension.
- **A non-elevated Windows run could not be made.** `runas /trustlevel` failed over SSH, so every
  Windows reading is from an elevated supervisor.

## Round 2

- **"Every process" means every pid, and that has to be designed in before the run.**
  - Round 1 recorded only the long-lived processes, and the judge was right that synchronous
    helpers and M4's parent are processes too.
  - Round 2 recorded each spawn in Node, made the fixture write its own pids, and ran a WMI
    process-start watcher on Windows.
  - Even so, the measuring sessions themselves cannot be fully recorded, and the report names what
    is left.
- **A watcher must outlive the events it is meant to catch.** Check A stopped the Windows watcher
  before WMI had delivered check A's own start events.
- **A command log is cheapest to produce from the session record.** Round 1's hand-kept
  appendix could only give time windows. The retained tool-call record gives exact inputs, times
  and outputs.

// ABOUTME: Card #1458: combines the as-written process records into one table, and checks each entry was written before the
// transcript's next command on its host after the process started.
'use strict';
const fs = require('node:fs');
const path = require('node:path');
const E = path.resolve(__dirname, '../../docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object');
const T = fs.readFileSync(path.join(E, 'transcript.txt'), 'utf8');
const cmds = [...T.matchAll(/^### (\S+) -> (\S+)  host=([^\n]*?)  runner/gm)].map((m) => ({ start: m[1], end: m[2], host: m[3] }));
const iso = (s) => new Date(s.replace(/(\.\d{3})\d+Z$/, '$1Z')).getTime();
const lst = (s) => new Date(s + ' GMT-0600').getTime();
const rows = [];
for (const l of fs.readFileSync(path.join(E, 'process-record-laptop.jsonl'), 'utf8').trim().split('\n')) {
  const e = JSON.parse(l); rows.push({ file: 'process-record-laptop.jsonl', host: 'laptop', pid: e.pid, created: e.created, createdMs: lst(e.created), recorded: e.recorded, by: e.by, how: 'step runner spawn', what: e.what });
}
const runs = path.join(E, 'hostpc-runs');
for (const r of fs.readdirSync(runs).sort()) for (const f of fs.readdirSync(path.join(runs, r)).filter((x) => x.endsWith('.jsonl')).sort()) {
  for (const l of fs.readFileSync(path.join(runs, r, f), 'utf8').trim().split('\n').filter(Boolean)) {
    const e = JSON.parse(l); rows.push({ file: `hostpc-runs/${r}/${f}`, host: 'hostpc', pid: e.pid, created: e.created, createdMs: iso(e.created), recorded: e.recorded, by: e.by, how: e.how, what: e.what });
  }
}
const out = ['ABOUTME: Card #1458 process record, combined: every entry of the as-written records, with host, pid, creation time and the UTC time',
  'it was written, and whether it was written before the transcript\'s next command on that host after the process started.', '',
  'Generated by gen-record.cjs from process-record-laptop.jsonl (written by the step runner t.cjs on the laptop) and hostpc-runs/<run>/rec-*.jsonl',
  '(written on the Windows machine by the launcher, the supervisor, the fixture and its members, as each process started).',
  'Creation time: on the laptop, ps -o lstart= in the laptop\'s local time (CST, UTC-6), to the second; on hostpc, GetProcessTimes, in UTC, to 100 ns.',
  'Recorded: the writer\'s clock, UTC. "next command" is the first transcript entry on that host that starts after the creation time (laptop clock).',
  'Every transcript entry is a laptop command; those whose host names hostpc are also hostpc commands. The hostpc clock read about 0.4 to 0.7 s behind the',
  'laptop clock (compare each launcher\'s first line with its entry\'s start), which makes every hostpc margin below smaller than the true one by that much, not larger.', ''];
let bad = 0;
for (const r of rows) {
  const next = cmds.find((c) => (r.host === 'laptop' || c.host.includes('hostpc')) && iso(c.start) > r.createdMs);
  const ok = !next || iso(r.recorded) < iso(next.start); if (!ok) bad++;
  out.push(`${r.host.padEnd(7)} pid ${String(r.pid).padEnd(6)} created ${String(r.created).padEnd(30)} recorded ${r.recorded}  next command ${next ? next.start : '(none after it)'}  ${ok ? 'earlier: yes' : 'earlier: NO'}  | ${r.how} by ${r.by} | ${r.file} | ${r.what}`);
}
const pairs = new Set(rows.map((r) => `${r.host}:${r.pid}:${r.created}`));
out.push('', `entries: ${rows.length}; distinct (host, pid, creation time): ${pairs.size}; entries written later than the next command: ${bad}`);
fs.writeFileSync(path.join(E, 'process-record.txt'), out.join('\n') + '\n');
console.log(out.slice(-1)[0]);
EOF

## D13 — macOS is v0's only host

**Status:** Ratified.

### Rule

1. v0 runs the engine on macOS, and writes no Windows-specific code before the WSL2 spike
   reports. WSL2 is the route it tries first.
2. What Rigger builds is unconstrained by this. A consumer on macOS can build software that runs
   anywhere.

### Deferred, and what returns it

| Deferred | Returns when |
|---|---|
| A native Windows host, with its own containment backend | The WSL2 spike reports that Engineer workloads do not run under WSL2, or a consumer needs a host Rigger cannot reach through WSL2. |

### Notes

One host keeps the containment mechanics to one process model. Process groups, survivor kills and
worktree paths each behave differently on Windows, and every one of them sits in the core, where a
bug costs a stray process or a lost result.

WSL2 is the cheaper route because it reuses the macOS model rather than adding a second one. The
spike exists because that reuse is an assumption, not a finding.

## D14 — Critical is what a maker revision cannot resolve

**Status:** Ratified.

### Rule

1054:## Failure model
1611:process that leaves its group is outside this containment. L0 never stops a process group to contain

{
 "file_path": "/Users/cjwilliams/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1458-spike-engineer/docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object.md",
 "content": "ABOUTME: Spike findings for card #1458: whether a Windows supervisor running below High Mandatory Level can hold an agent's\nwhole process tree in a Job Object and end it, both by ending the job and after the supervisor is killed outright.\n\n# A non-elevated Windows supervisor and a Job Object\n\n## The answer\n\nYes, for both endings, on this host. A supervisor ran as a dedicated standard account at Medium\nMandatory Level. It created a Job Object, set kill-on-job-close on it and assigned the fixture root to it.\nEvery call returned success, in all four runs that reached the job. That held even though the\nsupervisor was already in a job before it created its own.\n\n- **E1, `TerminateJobObject`.** It ended all five fixture members (M1 to M4 and M6) and the root, in\n  both runs (n3 and n4). The job reported no live process 0.350 ms and 0.458 ms after the call. A\n  listing 12 s later found none of them.\n- **E2, the supervisor killed with `taskkill /F`, kill-on-job-close set.** It ended every member\n  and the root, in both runs (n5 and n6). A listing 12 s after the kill found none of them. In n6,\n  none of the recorded tree was alive 14.6 ms after `taskkill` returned. The SSH session that\n  started the supervisor was open at both post-kill listings, so both runs count.\n\nThis removes the condition #1430 attached to its Windows recommendation. #1430 measured an elevated\nsupervisor only (\"Native Windows: a Job Object per dispatch, with kill-on-job-close\"). Here a\nstandard account's Medium-integrity token ran the same calls, and they behaved the same.\n\nTwo things limit the answer. The supervisor reached the account through `CreateProcessWithLogonW`,\nin session 0 and a window station the launcher made, not in an interactive desktop session. An\nadministrator's filtered (UAC) token was not tried. \"What the next card must not assume\" says why\neach matters.\n\n## What ran, and where\n\nEvery run was on the Windows machine (`hostpc`). The laptop only drove it over SSH. The WSL2 machine\nand the Mac mini were not used.\n\n| Run | Ending | Outcome |\n|---|---|---|\n| n1 | E1 | Failed before any attempt: the launcher threw while reading its own token. No account process started (\"What failed\") |\n| n2 | E1 | `CreateProcessWithLogonW` succeeded, and the account's process exited at once with `0xC0000142`. No supervisor ran (\"What failed\") |\n| n3, n4 | E1, `TerminateJobObject` | Measured |\n| n5, n6 | E2, `taskkill /F` of the supervisor, kill-on-job-close set | Measured |\n\nThe route had three parts:\n\n- **The launcher**, `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/win/launcher.cjs`, ran as `user` in an\n  SSH session that `ssh hostpc` opened. It started the supervisor as the account. It also recorded\n  every process of the supervisor's tree, and for E2 it killed the supervisor and listed.\n- **The supervisor**, `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/win/supervisor.cjs`, ran as `fx1458`.\n  It read its own token, created and assigned the job, listed, and then ended the job (E1) or\n  waited to be killed (E2).\n- **The fixture**, `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/win/fixture.cjs` and\n  `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/win/member.cjs`, is #1430's Windows fixture (\"The\n  fixture\", below).\n\nEach run was one command on the laptop, through the step runner\n`docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/t.cjs`. That command was\n`ssh hostpc node C:\\Users\\user\\1458-spike-engineer\\launcher.cjs <e1|e2> <run>`, with the account's\npassword on stdin.\n\n`docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/` holds the evidence:\n\n- `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/transcript.txt`, every command run through the step runner.\n- `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/session-log.txt`, every tool call in the spike engineer's\n  retained session record (\"The transcript\").\n- `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/process-record.txt`, the process record, combined from the\n  as-written records in `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/process-record-laptop.txt` and\n  `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/hostpc-runs/`.\n- `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/hostpc-runs/`, each run's records and the supervisor's log,\n  copied from the Windows machine.\n- `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/`, the code that ran, in its final form (\"The code that\n  ran\").\n\n## Hosts and versions\n\nEach value is quoted from the command that printed it. Every command is in\n`docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/transcript.txt`, in the entry that starts at the time given.\n\n| What | Value | Printed by |\n|---|---|---|\n| Windows build | `Microsoft Windows [Version 10.0.26300.9974]` | `cmd /c ver`, entry 02:58:15.497Z |\n| Windows release, as the supervisor read it | `os.release() 10.0.26300` | the supervisor's `versions:` line, entries 03:04:35.647Z (n3) to 03:06:27.061Z (n6) |\n| Node | `node --version: v24.19.0`, and the supervisor's own `node v24.19.0 x64` | entry 02:58:15.497Z, and each supervisor's `versions:` line |\n| npm | `npm --version: 11.17.0` | entry 02:58:15.497Z |\n| koffi, the package the supervisor loaded | `koffi 3.3.8 (C:\\Users\\user\\1458-spike-engineer\\node_modules\\koffi\\package.json)`, `koffi.version 3.3.8` | the supervisor's `versions:` line in n4, n5 and n6 |\n| koffi's prebuilt binary package | `@koromix/koffi-win32-x64 3.3.8 (C:\\Users\\user\\1458-spike-engineer\\node_modules\\@koromix\\koffi-win32-x64\\package.json)` | the same line, n4 to n6. n3 printed `@koromix/koffi-win32-x64 3.3.8` |\n\nThe supervisor loaded no other package. In n3 its `versions:` line printed\n`koffi not loaded (ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED)`, because koffi's `package.json` is not in its\nexports map. From n4 on, the supervisor read each `package.json` from the package's directory\ninstead. The same install served every run.\n\nThe laptop ran the step runner under Node v26.13.0 and drove the PC with `OpenSSH_10.5p1`. Both\nvalues were printed outside the step runner, at the start of the session, so they are in\n`docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/session-log.txt` and not the transcript.\n\n## How the non-elevated token was obtained\n\n### The account\n\nI created **one** dedicated local standard account, **`fx1458`**, under the owner's ruling\n([1453#7073093965](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1453#issuecomment-7073093965), ruling\n2). The stated-intent comment named it before it was created:\n[1458#7073260684](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1458#issuecomment-7073260684), posted at\n2026-11-22T02:59:50Z, as the forge's `created_at` reports it. The account was created at\n03:00:02Z, by entry 03:00:03.059Z:\n\n```\n$u = New-LocalUser -Name fx1458 -Password (ConvertTo-SecureString $p -AsPlainText -Force) -Description 'Rigger card 1458 spike, temporary' -AccountNeverExpires\n```\n\nIt printed `SID : [SID withheld]` and `PrincipalSource : Local`.\n`groups containing fx1458:` then listed no group. The account was in no group, Administrators\nincluded. The password was generated on the laptop and sent to each command on stdin. The\ntranscript records that stdin as a secret and never records its value. The file that held it was\ndeleted at the end.\n\n### Why this route\n\nSSH as the account was not possible without a host-configuration change. The probe at 02:58:15Z\nprinted these lines from the Windows machine's `sshd_config`:\n\n```\nPasswordAuthentication no\nAuthenticationMethods publickey\nAllowUsers user\nAuthorizedKeysFile __PROGRAMDATA__/ssh/administrators_authorized_keys\n```\n\nChanging that file was outside the owner's ruling. An administrator's filtered token needs an\ninteractive logon as `user`, and the spike had no such logon. Windows OpenSSH gave `user` a full token,\nas #1430 found. The launcher's own token read:\n\n```\nTokenIntegrityLevel Mandatory Label\\High Mandatory Level (S-1-16-12288, RID 0x3000) | 1 (TokenElevationTypeDefault) | TokenIsElevated=1\n```\n\nSo the launcher, as `user`, started the supervisor as `fx1458` through the secondary logon service,\nwith `CreateProcessWithLogonW`. No profile was loaded (`dwLogonFlags` 0). The service, `seclogon`,\nwas already `Running` with start type `Manual` (entry 02:59:39.575Z), and the spike changed nothing\nabout it.\n\n### Each attempt, with its full output\n\nThere were three attempts. This card needs no name, because reaching an orphaned job is #1460's. A name\n  would have added a namespace permission that the card's question does not ask about.\n- Its listing reads processes in its own process, with no child. It takes a Toolhelp snapshot,\n  reads each command line and working directory from the PEB, takes the creation time from\n  `GetProcessTimes`, and checks membership with `IsProcessInJob`. #1430 ran PowerShell for its\n  listings.\n\n## Before any ending\n\n### Was the supervisor already in a job?\n\nIn every run, before it created its own job, the supervisor called\n`IsProcessInJob(GetCurrentProcess(), NULL, &r)`:\n\n```\nIsProcessInJob(GetCurrentProcess(), NULL) before creating a job: returned 1, Result=TRUE (already in a job)\n```\n\nThat line is the same in n3, n4, n5 and n6. The call returned 1. Windows sets the last error\nonly when a call fails, so that value is stale, left by an earlier call. That is a judgment from the\ndocumented contract. The card does not ask for this call's error.\n\n## E2, the supervisor killed outright with kill-on-job-close set (n5, n6)\n\n| Run | The kill | Listing at least 10 s later | Session open at that listing |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| n5 | `taskkill /F /PID 14732 (taskkill pid 8788, exit 0): SUCCESS: The process with PID 14732 has been terminated.`, logged at 03:05:57.277Z | at 03:06:09.330Z, 12.05 s later, `count=0` | yes |\n| n6 | `taskkill /F /PID 28892 (taskkill pid 36504, exit 0): SUCCESS: The process with PID 28892 has been terminated.`, logged at 03:06:31.958Z | at 03:06:44.001Z, 12.04 s later, `count=0` | yes |\n\nn5's post-kill listing (entry 03:05:52.331Z):\n\n```\nlisting run=n5 12 s after taskkill at 2026-11-22T03:06:09.330Z on Windows by pid 8860 (Toolhelp snapshot + PEB command line and CurrentDirectory + GetProcessTimes + IsProcessInJob)\nPID     PPID    CREATED (UTC, GetProcessTimes)    IN_SPIKE_JOB IN_ANY_JOB CWD | ROLE\ncount=0\n```\n\nM1, M2, M3, M4 and M6, and the root, were dead in both runs. The launcher found no survivor to end.\n\nn6 also timed the deaths (entry 03:06:27.061Z):\n`none of the 8 recorded tree processes (supervisor excepted) alive 14.6 ms after taskkill returned (first poll that found none)`.\nThat figure is **measured**. Both endpoints come from the launcher's `process.hrtime`: one reading\nwhen `taskkill` returned, and one at the first 5 ms poll that found none of the eight alive. The\neight are the root, M1 to M4, M6 and two conhost.exe. That launcher ran in the entry that starts at\n03:06:27.061Z. n5 ran before this timing was added.\n\n### Was the starting session open?\n\nThe session that started the supervisor is the launcher's SSH session. In each E2 run the launcher\nprinted its ancestry, up to the per-connection `sshd.exe`, just before and just after the post-kill\nlisting. In n5:\n\n```\n2026-11-22T03:06:09.298Z launcher ancestry at the post-kill listing: 8860:node.exe(created 2026-11-22T03:05:51.7026673Z, alive) <- 23216:cmd.exe(created 2026-11-22T03:05:51.6867963Z, alive) <- 31368:sshd.exe(created 2026-11-22T03:05:51.6314929Z, alive) <- 35400:sshd.exe(created 2026-11-22T03:05:51.4790525Z, alive) <- 4156:sshd.exe(created 2026-11-10T18:59:53.8544042Z, alive)\n2026-11-22T03:06:09.342Z launcher ancestry after the post-kill listing: 8860:node.exe(created 2026-11-22T03:05:51.7026673Z, alive) <- 23216:cmd.exe(created 2026-11-22T03:05:51.6867963Z, alive) <- 31368:sshd.exe(created 2026-11-22T03:05:51.6314929Z, alive) <- 35400:sshd.exe(created 2026-11-22T03:05:51.4790525Z, alive) <- 4156:sshd.exe(created 2026-11-10T18:59:53.8544042Z, alive)\n```\n\nn6 printed the same at 03:06:43.977Z and 03:06:44.014Z, around its listing at 03:06:44.001Z, with\nits connection's `sshd.exe` 17548 and 38448 alive. The transcript shows the same thing from the\nlaptop. Each run was one `ssh` command, and each command's output includes its post-kill listing.\nn5's entry ran from 03:05:52.331Z to 03:06:11.290Z, and n6's from 03:06:27.061Z to 03:06:46.028Z.\nSo both sessions were open when their listings were taken, and both E2 runs count toward the\nconclusion. No E2 run is unattributed.\n\n### What else could have ended the members\n\nThe supervisor was already in a job (\"Was the supervisor already in a job?\"), and the members were\nin that outer job too, since a nested job's processes are in its parent. That the spike's\nkill-on-job-close ended them, not the outer job, is a judgment. Its premises:\n\n- The members died within 14.6 ms of the supervisor's death (n6), and the supervisor held the\n  spike's job's only handle.\n- The outer job's holder was not the supervisor, and nothing else ended at that moment: the launcher\n  and its SSH session stayed alive.\n- In #1430's elevated x4, members outlived a supervisor killed without kill-on-job-close.\n\nThe run that would settle it is the same E2 without kill-on-job-close under this route. That is\n#1460's cell, outside this card.\n\n## The conclusion\n\nOn the evidence above, a supervisor running below High Mandatory Level can end a dispatch's whole\ntree through a Job Object, on this host:\n\n- **E1:** yes. n3 and n4 each ended the root and all five members, M1 to M4 and M6, by\n  `TerminateJobObject`, in under 0.5 ms.\n- **E2:** yes. n5 and n6 each ended the root and all five members when the supervisor was killed\n  with `taskkill /F`, through kill-on-job-close. The starting session was open at both post-kill\n  listings.\n\n## Recommendation\n\n#1430's Windows recommendation stands, without its elevation condition: a Job Object per dispatch,\nwith kill-on-job-close, created from the supervisor's own process.\n\nThe next card should do these things:\n\n- Create the job unnamed, from the supervisor's own process. A name is needed only to reach an\n  orphaned job, which is #1460's question.\n- Set `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`, and assign the dispatch's root before it starts\n  children, gated as the fixture was.\n- Expect the supervisor to be in a job already, and rely on nesting. Every run here nested.\n- Open the root with rights the same account holds. Here the supervisor used `PROCESS_ALL_ACCESS`\n  on its own child.\n\n### What the next card must not assume\n\n- **That an interactive session behaves the same.** Every supervisor here ran in session 0, in a\n  window station the launcher made, started by the secondary logon service. A developer's\n  terminal runs in an interactive session, under a job its terminal or IDE may have made. The Job\n  Object calls on a process's own children need no privilege, so the same result is expected\n  there, but that is a judgment and was not measured.\n- **That an administrator's filtered token behaves the same.** A filtered token is the usual one\n  when a developer who is an administrator runs Rigger without elevation. It was not tried, for the\n  reasons under \"Why this route\". It carries the same Medium label, so the same result is expected,\n  again a judgment.\n- **That any outer job permits nesting.** This host's outer job did. An outer job that a host,\n  terminal or CI runner makes with limits that conflict with the spike's could refuse\n  `AssignProcessToJobObject`. None was met here.\n- **That the outer job is benign.** #1430's caution stands: under SSH, the session's own job ended\n  every member on disconnect.\n\n### What would reverse it\n\n- A Medium-integrity supervisor in an interactive session, or under a filtered administrator token,\n  that gets a failure from `CreateJobObjectW`, `SetInformationJobObject` or\n  `AssignProcessToJobObject`.\n- An outer job, under the host Rigger will run on, that refuses the nested assignment.\n- The same E2 run without kill-on-job-close (#1460's cell) leaving the members dead. That would mean\n  the outer job, not the spike's, ended them in n5 and n6.\n- An agent tool that starts processes with `CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB` under a job that allows it\n  (#1430's reversal, unchanged).\n\n## What this would change, as a proposal\n\nThis section is a proposal for the PM, the architect and the owner. This pull request changes none\nof these. The finding removes a condition from #1430's proposal. It adds no new change of its own.\n\n| Document | What the recommendation would change |\n|---|---|\n| `D13` | Rule 1 makes macOS v0's only host. Its deferred row, \"A native Windows host, with its own containment backend\", is the row a Job Object backend would return under. This report adds that the backend does not need elevation on this host |\n| `R-STATE-17` | Containment is a process group plus a census of the dispatch's directory. A job would be a containment unit that also holds M6, which that definition leaves outside, as #1430 proposed |\n| `R-STATE-9` and `R-STATE-10` | The killed-outright path. Kill-on-job-close ends the tree when the supervisor dies, so on Windows nothing would remain for the next start. That holds without elevation too |\n| `ARCHITECTURE.md`, \"Failure model\" | L0's containment paragraph: \"A process that leaves its group is outside this containment\" |\n\n## What failed\n\n- **n1: the launcher threw before its first call.** `GetTokenInformation` fails with\n  `ERROR_BAD_LENGTH` unless a fixed-size class gets exactly its size. The launcher passed 1024 bytes\n  for `TokenElevationType` and `TokenElevation`, got `null`, and threw. No account process started.\n  Its full output is in entry 03:03:35.116Z and is quoted above. The fix passed 4 bytes.\n- **n2: the account's process could not initialise.** `CreateProcessWithLogonW` returned 1. The\n  process exited with `0xC0000142` 14 ms later, wrote nothing, and started no child. The launcher's\n  tracker found no conhost for it. Its full output is in entry 03:03:46.876Z and is quoted above.\n  From n3, the launcher gave the account a window station and desktop of its own.\n- **n3's `versions:` line could not read koffi's version** (`ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED`). The run\n  itself was not repeated. n4 to n6 read it, and the install did not change between them.\n- **The first laptop sweep printed every process's command name** (entry 03:07:58.964Z). The names\n  identify the owner's applications, so its listing lines are redacted in the transcript, with the\n  original output's sha256. It was rerun without names at 03:08:07.053Z. Its result, `0` matches,\n  was the same.\n\nNo run's output is kept outside the transcript. Each failed or repeated run's full output is in its\ntranscript entry, apart from the one marked redaction above.\n\n## Custody\n\n### The transcript\n\n`docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/transcript.txt` holds every command run through the step\nrunner `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/t.cjs`. For each command it gives these:\n\n- the exact argv, as JSON;\n- a script it carried: for PowerShell, the script that `-EncodedCommand` decodes to, verbatim, with\n  its sha256;\n- the UTC start and end on the laptop clock, and the host;\n- the pid and creation time of the command's process;\n- its exit status, and its full output.\n\nA secret on stdin is recorded as a secret, with its length, and never its value. Every command run\non the Windows machine went through the step runner. The host label `laptop -> hostpc (user)` means\nan `ssh` or `scp` to the machine as `user`.\n\nIt carries two marked edits, each a line beginning `[REDACTED EDIT:` with the original's sha256:\n\n- Entry 02:58:15.497Z: the names of other accounts and profiles are withheld\n  from the account and profile listings.\n- Entry 03:07:58.964Z: the laptop process names are withheld.\n\n`docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/session-log.txt` holds every tool call in the spike engineer's\nretained session record, up to its generation. It covers the laptop commands outside the step\nrunner: making directories, generating the password, posting the comment, building the evidence,\nthe git commands. It applies the same name redaction, generated by\n`docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/gen-session-log.py`. The calls after its generation are\nthese:\n\n- the credential scan;\n- the last laptop sweep;\n- the record conversion and generation;\n- the checks, the commit and the push.\n\nThe scan and the last sweep are in the transcript. The rest have only the pull request as evidence.\n\nThe hostpc clock read at least 0.59 s behind the laptop clock. Each launcher's first line, on the\nhostpc clock, precedes its own command's start, on the laptop clock. So every timed figure here uses\none host's clock for both of its endpoints.\n\n### The code that ran\n\n`docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/` holds the code in its final form:\n\n- `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/win/win.cjs`, the koffi bindings and the listing;\n- `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/win/rec.cjs`, the process record;\n- `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/win/launcher.cjs`;\n- `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/win/supervisor.cjs`;\n- `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/win/fixture.cjs` and\n  `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/win/member.cjs`;\n- `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/win/sweep.cjs`;\n- the laptop's step runner, sweep and generators.\n\nThree files changed between runs. Its last line\nreads `entries written later than the next command: 0`. ### 66. 2026-11-22T03:15:46.069Z -> 2026-11-22T03:15:46.282Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Check quoted lines appear verbatim in the transcript"}
cd /Users/cjwilliams/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1458-spike-engineer && python3 - <<'EOF'
import re
r=open('docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object.md').read(); t=open('docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/transcript.txt').read()
blocks=re.findall(r'```\n(.*?)```',r,re.S); miss=0
for b in blocks:
    for l in b.split('\n'):
        if l.strip() and l not in t: miss+=1; print('BLOCK MISS:',l[:150])
for m in re.findall(r'`([^`\n]{12,})`',r):
    if m not in t and not m.startswith('docs/') and '/' not in m[:6]:
        print('SPAN?',m[:150])
print('block misses',miss)
EOF

 "content": "ABOUTME: Journal for #1458: whether a Windows supervisor below High Mandatory Level can end an agent's whole process\ntree through a Job Object, by ending the job and after being killed outright.\n\n# #1458 — A non-elevated Windows supervisor and a Job Object\n\n## What ran\n\nOn 2026-11-22 a throwaway supervisor ran on the Windows machine as a dedicated standard account,\n`fx1458`, at Medium Mandatory Level. The owner's ruling allowed the account, and it was deleted at\nthe end. The supervisor held #1430's fixture in a Job Object, and ended it by `TerminateJobObject`\n(two runs) and by being killed with `taskkill /F` under kill-on-job-close (two runs). The code stayed\nin the gitignored `spikes/`. Its final form, the transcript and the process record are with\n[the report](../spikes/non-elevated-job-object.md).\n\n## What we learned\n\n- **Elevation was never what made Job Objects work.** At Medium, every call returned success:\n  creating the job, setting kill-on-job-close and assigning the root. Both endings ended every\n  member, as they did elevated in #1430.\n- **Nested jobs are the normal case.** The supervisor was already in a job before it made its own,\n  in every run, and the assignment still succeeded.\n- **This host's sshd admits one key-holding administrator only.** `AllowUsers user`, public key only.\n  A second account cannot log in over SSH without a host-configuration change, so the account\n  was reached through the secondary logon service instead.\n- **A process started as another account in an SSH session cannot use that session's window\n  station.** It died at once with `0xC0000142`. A window station and desktop that the launcher\n  made, with a DACL naming the account, fixed it.\n\n## What failed\n\n- **`GetTokenInformation` refuses a fixed-size class unless given its exact size.** The first\n  launcher passed 1024 bytes for `TokenElevation` and threw before it started anything.\n- **koffi's `package.json` is not exported,** so `require('koffi/package.json')` fails. Read the file\n  from the package's directory instead.\n- **A listing that prints process names leaks what the owner runs.** The first laptop sweep was\n  redacted and rerun with pids and start times only. In a public repository, a sweep should print\n  only what it is asked to show.\n"

### 86. 2026-11-22T03:18:54.065Z -> 2026-11-22T03:18:54.183Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Read the pointer pattern"}
cd /Users/cjwilliams/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/1458-spike-engineer && sed -n 10,33p scripts/doc-reference-check.mjs
--- result ---
// own; every document, every level and every exemption comes out of the file.
export const CONFIG = 'doc-references.json';

/**
 * A pointer into a file at a line: a path with an extension, a colon, and a number.
 *
 * The extension is what keeps `node:fs`, a clock time and a clause opener out. A pointer is a
 * claim about where something sits, and the file it points into moves without telling the
 * document, so the resolver reads one as a finding rather than trying to verify it.
 */
const POINTER = /\b[\w.@-]+(?:\/[\w.@-]+)*\.[A-Za-z]{1,6}:\d+\b/g;

/** What doc-references.json declares: the checked documents, their levels, and the exemptions. */
export function documentChecking(root) {
  return JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(root, CONFIG), 'utf8'));
}

/** Every hand-typed path:line pointer in one document, with the line it sits on. */
export function pathLineLiterals(text) {
  return text.split('\n').flatMap((line, index) =>
    [...line.matchAll(POINTER)].map((found) => ({ line: index + 1, literal: found[0] })));
}

/**

### 91. 2026-11-22T03:19:30.823Z -> 2026-11-22T03:19:31.061Z  host=laptop  tool=Bash  {"description": "Review the session-open passage"}

its connection's sshd.exe 17548 and 38448 alive. The transcript shows the same thing from the
laptop. Each run was one `ssh` command, and each command's output includes its post-kill listing.
n5's entry ran from 03:05:52.331Z to 03:06:11.290Z, and n6's from 03:06:27.061Z to 03:06:46.028Z.
So both sessions were open when their listings were taken, and both E2 runs count toward the
conclusion. No E2 run is unattributed.

### What else could have ended the members

The supervisor was already in a job ("Was the supervisor already in a job?"), and the members were
in that outer job too, since a nested job's processes are in its parent. That the spike's
kill-on-job-close ended them, not the outer job, is a judgment. Its premises:

- The members died within 14.6 ms of the supervisor's death (n6), and the supervisor held the
  spike's job's only handle.
- The outer job's holder was not the supervisor, and nothing else ended at that moment: the launcher
  and its SSH session stayed alive.
- In #1430's elevated x4, members outlived a supervisor killed without kill-on-job-close.

The run that would settle it is the same E2 without kill-on-job-close under this route. That is
#1460's cell, outside this card.

The spike report for #1458 (docs only): `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object.md`, with its evidence in `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/`, a journal entry, and its `doc-references.json` entry.

**The answer.** Yes, for both endings, on the Windows machine. A supervisor ran as a dedicated standard account (`fx1458`) at Medium Mandatory Level (`S-1-16-8192`, read from its own token in each run). It created a Job Object, set kill-on-job-close and assigned the fixture root. Every call returned success, nested inside the job the supervisor already sat in.
- E1: `TerminateJobObject` ended the root and M1 to M4 and M6 in n3 and n4. The job read no live process after 0.350 ms and 0.458 ms, and the listing 12 s later read `count=0`.
- E2: `taskkill /F` of the supervisor ended them all through kill-on-job-close in n5 and n6. The starting SSH session was open at both post-kill listings.

This removes the elevation condition from #1430's Windows recommendation.

**Limits the report names.** The account was reached through `CreateProcessWithLogonW` from `user`'s SSH session. sshd admits only `user`, by key, and changing that was outside the ruling. So the supervisor ran in session 0 and a window station the launcher made, not on an interactive desktop. A filtered administrator token was not tried. Whether the outer job played any part in E2 is a judgment. The run that would settle it, E2 without kill-on-job-close, is #1460's cell.

**Custody.**
- The account was created under the owner's ruling, after the stated-intent comment [1458#7073260684](https://github.com/williacj/rigger/issues/1458#issuecomment-7073260684), and deleted at the end. The report quotes the listing afterwards.
- No other host-configuration change was made. The Mac mini was not used.
- Every Windows command ran through a step runner whose transcript records exact argv, scripts, UTC times, pids and full output.
- The process record has 158 entries for 82 processes, each written before the next command on its host.
- The final sweeps on both hosts match no record entry on pid and creation time.

**What failed, and is in the report:**
- n1: the launcher threw on a `GetTokenInformation` size.
- n2: the account's process died with `0xC0000142` in the SSH session's window station.

One thing is not in the report. The first commit attempt was refused because the worktree had no `node_modules`: every test failed with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` for `acorn`. `npm ci` fixed it, and the second attempt passed (`pass 6018`, `fail 0`).

No recorded decision, requirement or `ARCHITECTURE.md` section is changed. The report names `D13`, `R-STATE-17`, `R-STATE-9`/`R-STATE-10` and "Failure model" as a proposal only.

## R-LOOP-6: covered

The acceptance covers the question: actual below-High supervisor tokens, the three Job Object calls, membership and live-fixture premises, each ending and its time bound, the SSH-session confound, a separate conclusion and reversal evidence. ## Blocking findings

**F1 — The submitted transcript is incomplete (item 21).** Where: report lines 534–541 and `session-log.txt:3356–3359`. The report says that the record generation, report edits, checks, commit and push after the log was generated “have only the pull request as evidence.” The PR body further discloses a failed commit/check attempt followed by npm ci, neither of which is present in the submitted logs. A commit object proves an eventual commit; it supplies neither the missing command argv nor its UTC execution time and output. This violates the card's “Exact commands” rule and item 21. This PR introduces the omission.

Probe (read-only, rerunnable by changing the checkout argument):

```sh
TMPDIR=/Users/cjwilliams/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/judge-1468-codex-r1/tmp python3 /Users/cjwilliams/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/judge-1468-codex-r1/audit-evidence.py /Users/cjwilliams/GitHub/rigger-worktrees/judge-1468-codex-r1/repo
```

The retained `audit-evidence.log` reports the final session entry's missing result and zero occurrences of `npm ci` and `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` in both submitted logs. The report's own admission supplies the positive evidence that those commands occurred; the PR body supplies the failed attempt's reason. Found by reading, confirmed by the probe. **To make sound:** complete the committed transcript from contemporaneous records, including the omitted commands and their UTC times/hosts; distinguish anything that cannot be recovered rather than reconstructing commands from memory.

**F2 — A quoted executed command lacks its transcript entry (item 22).** Where: report lines 696–700. The report calls its awk/grep filter a command “run on the committed transcript.” Its exact text occurs in neither submitted log. Running it now gives empty output and exit 1, but that does not supply the missing historical provenance. This violates item 22 and the card's “Exact commands” rule. This PR introduces the unsupported assertion.

The same probe extracts the fenced command from the report and reports:

```text
ITEM 22 quoted awk command present in transcript: False
ITEM 22 quoted awk command present in supplementary session log: False
```

**To make sound:** provide the exact recorded execution with time/host, or remove the unrecorded quoted command from the report. Ensure every command the report continues to quote has its transcript entry. The underlying recursive scan is already retained; this finding does not allege a credential leak.

**F3 — Failed/repeated output is not complete (item 25).** Where: `transcript.txt:1427–1433`, report lines 499–504, and the PR body paragraph beginning “One thing is not in the report.” The repeated laptop sweep explicitly deletes 733 listing lines and substitutes a digest plus summary. The report says its unredacted copy was deleted. Separately, the failed commit/check attempt attributed to missing acorn has no full output under the evidence directory and no entry under “What failed.” A digest of unavailable bytes and a later green result do not meet the full-output obligation. This violates item 25, “Failed runs in full,” and the standing judge brief's missing-failed-output bar. This PR introduces the incomplete evidence.

The same probe prints the entire retained sweep entry, including:

```text
[REDACTED EDIT: this run listed the command name of every process on the laptop. 733 listing lines are removed, because they name the owner's applications; the command was rerun without names at 03:08:07Z. ...]
```

It also finds zero `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` occurrences in either submitted log. **To make sound:** retain and reference the required complete failed/repeated-run output, with the report explaining each failure/repetition. If a privacy redaction is to replace the author's explicit “full, unabridged” bar, obtain the appropriate explicit grant/author revision; the maker cannot grant that exception to itself. This finding does not request publication of private application information without that decision.

## Non-blocking findings

None. Interactive/filtered-token runs and a without-kill-on-close control are expressly outside this card; I did not turn those known limits into additional acceptance work.

## Custody

### The transcript

`docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/transcript.txt` holds every command run through the step
runner `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/t.cjs`. For each command it gives these:

- the exact argv, as JSON;
- a script it carried: for PowerShell, the script that `-EncodedCommand` decodes to, verbatim, with
  its sha256;
- the UTC start and end on the laptop clock, and the host;
- the pid and creation time of the command's process;
- its exit status, and its full output.

A secret on stdin is recorded as a secret, with its length, and never its value. Every command run
on the Windows machine went through the step runner. The host label `laptop -> hostpc (user)` means
an `ssh` or `scp` to the machine as `user`.

It carries two marked edits, each a line beginning `[REDACTED EDIT:` with the original's sha256:

- Entry 02:58:15.497Z: the names of other accounts and profiles are withheld
  from the account and profile listings.
- Entry 03:07:58.964Z: the laptop process names are withheld.

`docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/session-log.txt` holds every tool call in the spike engineer's
retained session record, up to its generation. It covers the laptop commands outside the step
runner: making directories, generating the password, posting the comment, building the evidence,
the git commands. It applies the same name redaction, generated by
`docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/gen-session-log.py`. It was generated at about 03:16Z. The calls after that are these:

- the credential scans, at 03:16:40Z, 03:17:04Z and 03:17:31Z;
- the laptop sweeps, at 03:17:09Z and 03:17:31Z;
- the record generation;
- the edits to this report, the checks, the commit and the push.

The scans and sweeps are in the transcript. The rest have only the pull request as evidence.

The hostpc clock read at least 0.59 s behind the laptop clock. Each launcher's first line, on the
hostpc clock, precedes its own command's start, on the laptop clock. So every timed figure here uses
one host's clock for both of its endpoints.
### The credential scan

The last scan ran at 03:17:31Z, over every file under `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/` (29
files), through the step runner. Its command is in entry 03:17:31.653Z:

```
$ ["grep","-rnIiE","gh[pousr]_[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}|github_pat_|sk-ant-|sk-[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}|AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}|xox[baprs]-|BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY|bearer [A-Za-z0-9._-]{20,}|(password|passwd|secret|token|api[_-]?key)[[:space:]]*[:=]","../../docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object"]
```

It printed 43 lines. Every one falls in one of these classes:

- The launcher's log label `own token:`. Each run's launcher line, and the code that prints it, has
  one.
- The koffi binding names `OpenProcessToken:` and `GetTokenInformation` in `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/win/win.cjs`.
- The step runner's variable `secret = `, in `docs/spikes/non-elevated-job-object/code/t.cjs` and where the session log and transcript show
  that code.
